# Dynamic Strategies of Machine Learning for Extenuation of Security Breaches in Wireless Sensor Networks

  
  
**Shweta Paliwal^([1](#9781681088624-C2.html#aff1), [\*](#9781681088624-C2.html#cor1))**  
¹ Department of Computer Science and Engineering, Meerut Institute of Engineering and Technology (MIET), Meerut, India  
  

## Abstract

Wireless sensor networks (WSNs) have turned up as a promising technology due to their deployment nature and the knock-off feature of cost-effectiveness. The rapid increase in the demand for wireless applications has opened up new security vulnerabilities which are related to their infrastructure-less nature and their nature of transmission and hence made WSNs the centre point of attraction for attackers and intruders. Machine Learning has embedded comprehensive solutions to accord with modern security breaches. It has gained a mark of deterrent technology by shoring up the infrastructure of security through mapping security breaches and performing the identification of unknown patterns. This chapter focuses on the illustration of security breaches in wireless sensor networks along with the glimpse of reduction in attacks through dynamic algorithms and strategies of Machine Learning. We have proposed some feature selection methodologies in order to identify the best features out of the available network dataset. These feature selection methods are evaluated against the existing machine learning classifiers in order to identify the best feature selection strategy and the best classifier.

**Keywords:** Cyber security, Feature selection, Machine learning, Wireless Sensor Networks (WSNs).  
  

* * *

^(\*) **Corresponding author Shweta Paliwal:** Department of Computer Science and Engineering, Meerut Institute of Engineering and Technology (MIET), Meerut, India; E-mail: [shwtplwl23@gmail.com](mailto:shwtplwl23@gmail.com)  
  

## 1\. INTRODUCTION

The tremendous growth in the field of Information Technology and Communication has paved the way for the evolution of Wireless Sensor Networks (WSNs). Today wireless sensor networks have marked their presence in various application areas of medicine, military, smart spaces, and many others. A wireless sensor network (WSN) is defined as a collection of spatially dispersed network devices along with dedicated sensors to monitor and record the physical condition

of the environment. Wireless sensor networks can either be demand-driven or event-driven. In event-driven sensor networks, the network triggers as soon as it senses changes in the monitored area, whereas, in demand-driven, the network serves as an updated database for a client. Wireless sensor networks are comprised of several tiny sensor nodes that are deployed either randomly or deterministically \[1\]. These nodes gather the data from the environment and transmit the sensed data to the base stations for further processing. WSNs provide cross-layer design and scalability along with the capacity to handle the failure of nodes. Different types of sensors such as seismic, magnetic, visual, *etc*. are embedded within the WSNs, thereby monitoring ambient situations.

The characterization of sensor nodes depends on their storage capacity, limited processing power, and limited energy. Based on the nature of nodes, wireless sensor networks are classified into two types of networks, namely homogeneous and heterogeneous sensor networks. A network where all sensor nodes have a similar property for communication, memory, energy, and reliability is termed a homogeneous network \[2\]. Due to the nature of transmission of wireless networks, security has become a critical issue. Not only security but the designers of wireless sensor networks must also focus on the issues related to the reliability of data, aggregation of data, and fault detection. Since the sensor nodes are capable of operating both in ad hoc networks as well as the normal networks, security goals could be categorized into two types; Primary Security Goals and Secondary Security Goals.

Machine learning provides low complexity estimation for the model of the system, thereby being deployed in a complex environment of the wireless sensor networks. Designers of wireless sensor networks are focusing on machine learning algorithms to gain new insights and knowledge about the unreachable location. It can be seen that wireless sensor networks are adopting machine learning to increase the security of the communication channel as machine learning is capable of dealing with the dynamic behavior of the wireless sensor networks.

The paper has identified different feature selection methodologies from the network dataset. Feature selection methodologies are important as they fasten up the training of the ML model and at the same time reduce the complexity of the model, thus providing easier interpretation. We have identified the best feature selection strategy and then evaluated it against several machine learning classifiers to identify the algorithm with optimized performance in the detection of security breaches.

Section 2 describes the security concerns in WSNS followed by a description of Machine learning in Section 3. Section 4 describes how ML addressed different security issues, and Section 5 describes the approaches that have been developed to combat these issues. The proposed methodology has been described in Section 6. Section 7 describes the conclusion of the entire work.

## 2\. SECURITY CONCERNS IN WIRELESS SENSOR NETWORKS

Evolution in the field of manufacturing industries and wireless communication networks has contributed to the growth of WSNs. They are made up of cost-effective sensor nodes that release a high amount of energy. Several characteristics make WSNs prone to security attack which includes resource-constrained nature of sensor nodes and ad hoc deployment of nodes. The basic requirement for WSNs to survive includes reliability, availability, and energy efficiency. Denial of service attacks and eavesdropping attacks targets the confidentiality and integrity of the data being transmitted over the network, and Flooding and Jamming attacks are related to the power consumption of the network \[3-4\]. Hence it can be stated that there are several security attacks that target the sensitive points of WSNs, such as Bandwidth, Power, and Routing Mechanism of WSNs, thus opening up new gateways for intruders. WSNs are more vulnerable to Man in the Middle-Security Attack as they provide complete control over information that is transmitted over the network. The attackers target the confidentiality and integrity of data.

 

### 2.1. Eavesdropping Attack

Eavesdropping attack forms the base for other attacks and is a serious security threat to wireless sensor networks. There are two sorts of eavesdropping attacks in wireless sensor networks; passive eavesdropping, where the information is detected by malicious nodes through the listening of message transmission in a wireless broadcasting medium. Active eavesdropping, where information is captured by malicious nodes disguising themselves to be authorized nodes through queries sent to the transmitter.

 

### 2.2. Jamming Attack

Jamming is considered as a special class of DOS (Denial of service) attack. In a Jamming attack, high-frequency radio signals are emitted that disrupt the operations of the transmitter. A jammer could either be a simple transmitter or an entire jamming station equipped with special equipment.

 

### 2.3. Tampering

In this attack, the original node is replaced by a fake node, and the intruder gets access to sensitive information like cryptographic keys. The attack usually occurs at the physical layer.

 

### 2.4. Exhaustion and Collision Attack

In this attack, MAC (Media Access Control) protocol is hampered by a malicious node through transmission over the channel making other nodes deprived of access to the channel. Time-division multiplexing is used as a countermeasure for this attack \[5\]. A collision attack occurs when an attempt is made to transmit a packet at the same frequency by two nodes.

 

### 2.5. Sybil Attack

WSNs are vulnerable to Sybil attacks. Here multiple identical nodes are created by a malicious node. Based upon the fabrication of identities, a Sybil attack can be launched in various ways in WSNs. An identical node could easily get access from the normal node and achieve the information during one-hop communication. This communication to unauthorized nodes results in the loss of data. Sybil attacks lead to the degradation of the integrity of data, security, and utilization of resources. Sybil attacks are classified into two categories:

**Stolen Identity Attack and Fabricated Attack:** New malicious nodes are created from the legal node. For example; the ID of 16 bits of an authorized sensor node can be used to create a fabricated node with the same ID of 16 bits.

**Direct and Indirect Attack:** Communication between Sybil nodes and real nodes is carried out directly, whereas, in indirect attack, it is carried out through a malicious node \[6\].

 

### 2.6. Blackhole Attack

In this attack, the data traffic is attracted to a malicious node. This malicious node disguised itself as the shortest route for absorption of packets. Once the nodes are captured, they are programmed again so that they can not transmit the data packets to the destination. Re programmed nodes are called “black-hole nodes”.

 

### 2.7. Wormhole Attack

The intruder tunnels the message received from one part of the network and transmits them to another part of the network through replaying at a low latency link. Hidden and exposed are the two modes in which the wormhole attack exists. A wormhole attack is a type of denial of service attack that can affect the network without considering the techniques of cryptography that have been implemented, thus making detection of an attack difficult \[7\].

 

### 2.8. Grayhole Attack

In this attack, the malicious node acts as a normal node in the network to drop the packets that are being transmitted over the network, thus hiding the information that needs to be sent out to the destiny node \[8\]. Gray holes in wireless sensor networks can be classified into two categories using the number of nodes in WSNs and the drop of packets.

 

### 2.9. Sinkhole Attack

It is a type of insider attack, where the intruder first converts a normal node to a compromised node, and then all the traffic is attracted towards the compromised node based on the routing metric of the routing protocol that is being used \[9-10\]. Communication pattern in wireless sensor networks makes the detection of sinkhole attack a great challenge.

 

### 2.10. Hello Flood Attack

A few nodes in wireless sensor networks broadcast a “hello” message to mark their presence to all the neighboring nodes. Hello flood attack can be caused out by such nodes so that it can be chosen as a parent node by a large number of nodes present in a network. In this attack, the intruder gets hold of a node and declares itself to be the neighbor of other nodes by sending out Hello messages. When a node receives this message it assumes that the node is in communication range and thus starts transmission with the captured node.

## 3\. MACHINE LEARNING EMERGING AS A SAFEGUARD TO WSNs

The term Machine Learning (ML) came into existence when the first-ever neural network was designed. Machine learning can be described as “designing the system with a capability to automatically learn and keeps on improving with experience without the need of being programmed explicitly”. In wireless sensor networks, machine learning will not only improve the performance but will also help in accessing the large amount of data that have been captured by the sensors. The sensor nodes required to cover a target area could be easily predicted using ML techniques. The categorization of existing machine learning techniques is based on the structure of the model and the ability to learn. Fig. (**[1](#9781681088624-C2.html#f1)**) describes the basic process behind the working of the ML model.

![](Images/9781681088624-C2_F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Design of machine learning model. 

In the domain of cybersecurity, machine learning is depicting progressive results by performing the analysis of previous scenarios and predicting the compelling measures for defense. There are certain scenarios where the technology is surpassing traditional security measures.

1.  Machine Learning is offering the identification of critical real-time incidents.
2.  Algorithms are capable of revealing advanced defense mechanisms by analyzing the network traffic and patterns of security breaches held out in past.
3.  Machine Learning can detect insider threats and violations in policy for the network Infrastructure.
4.  Advanced IDS have been designed through approaches of machine learning.
5.  Machine learning provides synchronization and congestion control for wireless sensor networks.

For wireless sensor networks, there are certain points where machine learning is offering advanced analytical solutions.

**Localization:** Sometimes, identification of sensor nodes in the infrastructure of WSN after its deployment becomes a critical task, for which reconfiguration is required. Algorithms of machine learning provide ease of classification for unknown and anchor nodes of the network. Moreover, cluster nodes are trained to find the specific node in a sensor network.

**Coverage in WSN:** Sensor nodes are responsible for covering the field of interest in a network. Machine Learning will let you know the minimum number of sensor nodes required to provide complete coverage for the network.

**Detection of Anomaly:** Data is accumulated continuously from the environment and thus transmitted to base stations at a continuous pace. Hence protection of this sensing data is a concern point; machine learning is implementing hybrid detection strategies for the identification of malicious data traffic over the network.

**Data Aggregation and Clustering:** It poses a challenge to transmit the data efficiently in a distributed large-scale sensor network. Machine learning performs compression of data at cluster heads through the extraction of similar and dissimilar functions.

**Query processing and Event detection:** Machine Learning algorithms help in the development of advanced detection strategies with limited system requirements.

Machine Learning has witnessed improvements in the learning process of the sensor network and a reduction in end-to-end delay transmission. It is also providing a significant decrease in the communication overhead through the application of dimensionality reduction algorithms at the sensor node. Algorithms are continuously advancing the synchronization of nodes and moderating themselves to adapt to the dynamic changing environment of the wireless network so that reconfiguration can be achieved easily.

## 4\. SUPERVISED LEARNING

These are the systems where the labeled input and output data are classified to form a basis of learning. Models based on supervised learning are used to represent learned relations between system parameters along with input and output. For wireless sensor networks, problems of routing, localization, fault detection, and synchronization are solved using machine learning techniques.

### 4.1. Linear Regression and Logistic Regression

It is a simple supervised learning method where a dependent value (y) is predicted based upon the independent variable (x). Regression can either be linear regression or logistic regression and could have continuous or quantitative variables. The mathematical notation of linear regression is represented below as equation 1.

| ![](Images/9781681088624-C2_equ1.jpg) || (1) |
| --- | --- |

Logistic Regression states that there exist only two possible classes which describe the nature of dependent or target variable. The dependent variable is defined in the form of 0 and 1.

Apart from these two, another regression is Bayesian Regression that uses probability estimates for the formulation of linear regression. The resultant variable is defined as y, which is generated from Gaussian distribution. Bayesian Regression helps in the determination of posterior probabilities and is not focused on finding the best solution.

Support Vector Regression is used for real value estimation and comes under the supervised learning approach. It is based on the usage of kernels and is used for the training of symmetrical functions. SVMs solve binary classification problems by formulating them as convex optimization problems.

 

### 4.2. Artificial Neural Networks (ANN)

It is a supervised-based learning model that functions on biological neural networks. ANN consists of a large number of processing units called neurons and operates on 3 layers, namely an input layer, a hidden layer, and an output layer. In wireless sensor networks, ANN is used to improve the efficiency of routing, congestion control, and aggregation of data. Artificial neural networks can memorize multiple outputs and boundaries of a decision in one go, hence are considered appropriate for resolving the challenges of a network, and they also come up with cost-effective solutions.

 

### 4.3. Decision Tree

Decision trees are based on if-then rules and consist of two nodes, namely decision nodes and leaf nodes. It is a supervised learning algorithm that splits the data set on the basis of different conditions. The leaf nodes of the decision tree represent class labels and the attributes are represented by the internal nodes of the tree. The attributes of the root node are selected through either using the process of the Gini Index or the Information Gain method. In wireless sensor networks, decision trees are used to deal with issues such as detection of anomalies, selection of mobile sink paths, *etc*. They also help in the identification of features such as corruption rate and loss rate.

### 4.4. Random Forest

Random forest algorithm is based on ensemble and comprised of a large number of decision trees. Random forest algorithm performs its operation in two stages; First the creation of a random forest classifier and second the prediction of results. The Random forest can be used for problems based on either classification or regression. In a random forest algorithm, it is considered that “higher the number of trees, higher the accuracy in predicted results,” thus reducing the level of sensitivity when compared with other machine learning techniques. With this algorithm, there will be no problem of overfitting, and it also helps in feature engineering. The issues related to Media Access Control (MAC) have been resolved using the random forest algorithm.

### 4.5. Bayesian Learning

Bayesian learning is supervised learning based on statistical model parameters being considered as random variables and the results are drawn using posterior distribution. A Bayesian inference usually considers a small number of training samples and follows the Bayes rule given by equation 2.

| ![](Images/9781681088624-C2_equ2.jpg) || (2) |
| --- | --- |

### 4.6. Support Vector Machine (SVM)

SVM is a supervised learning approach where each data item is plotted as a point in n-dimensional space with the value of each feature being the value of a particular coordinate. Thus we can say that SVM is a classifier that suggests the optimal hyperplane for the categorization of data, and in two-dimensional spaces, this hyperplane is considered as the line. In wireless sensor networks, SVM can be used to detect the malicious behavior of the node and the connectivity problem. SVM works well when the data is either in unstructured form or in semi-structured form. The complexity of the SVM model is not affected by the number of features encountered in the training data.

### 4.7. K- Nearest Neighbor (K-NN)

K-NN is a supervised learning instance-based learning method used to deal with the problems of classification and regression. Euclidean distance, Manhattan distance, and Minkowski distance are the functions of distance used in the KNN method. The size of the input dataset is one of the parameters on which the complexity of KNN depends.

## 5\. UNSUPERVISED LEARNING

It is a learning method where the algorithms are trained using information that is neither trained nor classified. The information is categorized into groups by the system on the basis of similarities and differences between them. The systems based on unsupervised learning are capable of performing more complex tasks as compared to supervised learning methods. In wireless sensor networks, unsupervised learning is used for the clustering of nodes and tackling the issues of connectivity and routing.

 

### 5.1. K- Means Clustering

K means clustering partitions the n objects into k clusters where the objects belong to the cluster that has the nearest mean value. The main objective of K means clustering is to minimize the intracluster variance. The algorithm firstly considers the random locations of the K number and forms the clusters with the data points. Once the cluster has been formed, a new centroid is recalculated from each cluster. The process continues until there are no more changes in the value of centroid of all the clusters. This clustering method helps in the selection of optimal cluster heads for routing in wireless sensor networks.

 

### 5.2. Fuzzy C- Means Clustering

Fuzzy C means clustering was first developed by Dunn in 1973, and was later improved in 1981 by Bezdek, and is used in pattern recognition. Here each data point is assigned a membership function corresponding to each cluster center. The membership is assigned on the basis of the distance between the cluster of the center and the data point. In the case of overlapped datasets, optimal clustering is offered by Fuzzy c means clustering as compared to K means clustering.

## 6\. SEMI-SUPERVISED LEARNING

Algorithms based on semi-supervised learning are comprised of both labeled and unlabeled data set, thus it can be said that semi-supervised learning is a combination of supervised and unsupervised learning. The ultimate goal of semi-supervised learning is to classify the unlabelled data using some labeled set of information. Application areas of semi-supervised learning include speech analysis, protein sequence classification, classification of web content, and spam filtering. Semi-supervised learning has been used in wireless sensor networks for addressing the issue of localization.

## 7\. REINFORCEMENT LEARNING

Reinforcement learning is described as the capability of taking suitable action for maximization of reward in a particular environment. It is a learning methodology that enables an agent (can be a sensor node for WSNs) to learn by interacting with its environment. Q learning is one of the popular techniques of reinforcement learning. Q learning lets an agent know what actions need to be performed in a particular situation and could handle the problems with stochastic transitions. The routing problem in the wireless sensor networks has been reduced to an extent using the Q learning technique.

## 8\. MACHINE LEARNING ADDRESSING ISSUES in WSNs

### 8.1. Machine Learning Addressing Issue of Security

Securing the wireless sensor networks against different attacks (discussed in the above section) is a challenging issue. However machine learning has emerged as a safeguard against these attacks, researchers have proposed several techniques that use algorithms of machine learning for the detection and prevention of attacks on WSNs. An intrusion detection system (IDS) plays an important part in the detection of malicious nodes in wireless sensor networks. Parras *et al*. \[11\] designed a Markov decision process-based framework to deal with the problem of ad-hoc defense and optimality using reinforcement learning. The approach is tested against spectrum sensing attack in a wireless sensor network

In order to improve the fusion classification, four classification techniques namely Enhanced K – nearest Neighbor (EKNN), Enhanced Extreme Learning Machine (EELM), Enhanced Support Vector Machine (ESVM), and Enhanced Recurrent Extreme Learning Machine (ERELM) were proposed by Javaid *et al*. \[12\]. A total of twenty datasets were prepared and simulated using MATLAB. These proposed techniques give better accuracy and detection than the existing techniques.

These researchers have designed the approaches and then tested them against several subdivisions of the datasets. They have shown significant improvements in an overall analysis of performance measures that include the accuracy of the detection rate and other standard performance measures. A synthetic minority oversampling technique (SMOTE) for the balancing of datasets and Random Forest algorithm is applied for detection of intrusion in the wireless sensor networks by Tan *et al*. \[13\]. SMOTE is a technique proposed to resolve the problem of class imbalance and overfitting which is caused by non-heuristic oversampling. KDD-99 data set has been used which comprises forty-one attributes that were later divided into five categories of attacks. This research work concluded that a combination of random forest along with SMOTE has resulted in an accuracy of 92.57%. The protocols were verified using the AVISPA tool and HLPSL language. AVISPA stands for automated validation of Internet security Protocols and applications and has been used for the verification of protocols concerning authentication and Denial of service attacks by Chandre *et al*. \[14\].

A hybrid framework for the detection of a black hole and sinkhole attack in WSN was designed. The framework is based on the Proportional Overlapping Score (POS), and Minkowski K means to reduce the complexity and increase the detection accuracy. Features in the dataset are reduced and the data is converted into a preprocessed form using POS. Minkowski K means algorithm is used for the training and classification. The framework has achieved a high detection rate and low packet loss rate. It has also minimized the communication overhead in the network and the results are simulated using network simulator 2. The detection accuracy was 85.93% for Minkowski K means. Kumar *et al*. \[15\] proposed a detection framework that captures the sinkhole and black hole attacks and thus activates the normal sensor nodes through alert messages.

Intrusion detection systems are used for the detection of malicious behavior of nodes in a network. IDS for the detection uses a statistical tool based on binary logistic regression. This system takes node parameters for malicious behavior as input. This m- ID was deployed in real-time using various network topologies. Ioannou *et al*. \[16\] proposed twenty-four malicious scenarios for each network topology and attack. Later a machine learning technique for the detection of spoofing attacks in wireless sensor networks was developed. Two machine learning classifiers were hybridized to process and analyze the samples of signals for the detection of attacks. The K means algorithm and K-NN algorithm were the machine learning algorithms used in this technique. De *et al*. \[17\] proposed an algorithm for optimization of scenarios where the attacking and legitimate nodes are either at the same distance or placed at a very close distance from one another. This approach resulted in a 10% increase in the accuracy as compared to one of the existing technique

Qu *et al*. \[18\] designed a knowledge-based intrusion detection strategy (KBIDS) to bridge out the gap between detection rate and false alarm rate. Mean Shift Clustering Algorithm has been used for differentiating the abnormal patterns from the network traffic and support vector machine to minimize the classification error. The QUAL Net was used as a network simulator where 100 sensor nodes were randomly deployed in each region. KBIDS has provided the highest detection rate when compared with the existing six different models.

Outlier detection forms an important part of security concerns in wireless sensor networks. A survey on the outlier detection using machine learning techniques is conducted and it has been observed that Bayesian classification has emerged as the best approach for the detection by Dwivedi *et al*. \[19\]. A novel detection approach for the detection of intrusion based on a support vector machine is designed. Here genetic algorithm has been used for feature selection. The wireless network is simulated using a network simulator and parameters such as packet delivery were taken into consideration. Vijayanand *et al*. \[20\] proposed a feature selection technique and observed that the proposed genetic algorithm-based feature selection technique provides higher accuracy, less overhead, and computational complexity.

Random Forest algorithm has been used for the detection of DOS attacks in wireless sensor networks. Park *et al*. \[21\] proposed algorithm is compared against Artificial Neural Network based model and it has been observed that the proposed random forest algorithm has shown 99% accuracy for black hole attack, 96% for flooding attack, and 96% for normal and scheduling attacks. Later on, an algorithm was proposed for the detection of wormhole attacks in wireless sensor networks that reduced the rate of drop-in packets. A dynamic threshold value is considered for the detection of wormhole attacker nodes followed by the clustering technique for the selection of optimistic communication paths. Simulation of WSNs coding organization is performed using MATLAB, thus reducing the overall packet dropping and improving the performance of WSNs by Harpal *et al*. \[22\].

There exists one more category of denial of service attacks known as Vampire attacks. These attacks are designed to drain the power of the battery from the nodes and are difficult to detect as they are protocol compliant. Afterward, a neural network-based detection algorithm for the detection of compromised nodes in Vampire attack is proposed by Tanguturi *et al*. \[23\]. The data is divided into three categories training, validation, and testing. Performance metric mean square node was used for the performance evaluation of the designed detection node. The approach provided low errors and a high detection rate.

Researchers designed a classification and prediction model using Logistic Regression, Naïve Bayes, Support Vector Machine, and Random forest. These machine learning algorithms were tested against the KDD data set. It has been observed that Random forest has achieved the highest true positive rate (TPR) and provided an accuracy of 99% compared with other algorithms \[24\].

The research work is focused on a specialized dataset that provides detection and classification of four types of attacks on wireless sensor networks named as a black hole, gray whole, flooding, and scheduling attacks. LEACH hierarchical protocol has been used for routing. Twenty-three features were processed and simulated using network simulator 2. Artificial Neural Network is used for training the datasets, detection, and classification of DOS attacks. It has been concluded by Almomani *et al*. \[25\] that detection is 92.8% accurate for black hole attack, 99.4% for flooding attack, 92.2% for scheduling attack, and 99.8% for gray hole attack.

A hybrid anomaly detection scheme for the detection of the wormhole and black hole attacks using K-Means Clustering has been proposed. Wazid *et al*. \[26\] have used OPNET modeler for the creation of a WSN dataset and divided the sensor networks into clusters. The wireless sensor network is simulated under the normal traffic and attack scenarios using the OPNET simulator. They concluded that this hybridized approach provides a 98.6% detection rate with a false positive rate of 1.2%

 

### 8.2. Machine Learning Addressing Issue of Routing in Wireless Sensor Network

Expansion in need of massive video streaming creates an environment for effective routing transmission for maintenance of load balancing. A routing-based load balancing framework using Principal Component Analysis was designed and then the network has been trained on Neural Network. The framework proposed by Yao *et al*. \[27\] provided better throughput and delay.

Researchers stated that intelligent routing is needed for the internet of things WSNs therefore they developed an approach using the neuro-fuzzy rule cluster approach. This routing algorithm provided better network performance in accordance with energy utilization and packet utilization by Thangamarya *et al*. \[28\]. Later a reinforcement learning-based routing algorithm was proposed for the optimization. It uses a fuzzy link for cost estimation. The algorithm proposed provided significant improvements for the dynamic environment by Singh *et al*. \[29\]. Harmony routing-based energy-efficient algorithm is designed for addressing the issue of energy consumption in sensor nodes. The local strategy has been developed for improving the performance measure of the accuracy of the algorithm by Zeng *et al*. \[30\].

 

### 8.3. Data Aggregation in WSNs with Machine Learning

Apart from these, there are several other issues in a wireless sensor network that has been addressed through approaches of machine learning. Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C2.html#t1) presents a few more work done in the field of wireless sensor networks through strategies of machine learning.

Hence so far it has been observed that Machine Learning is successfully dealing with and mitigating the threats related to wireless sensor networks. The scope of the algorithms is not only limited to mitigation of security breaches but they are also dealing with issues of node localizations, routing, sensor management, and various others. If we talk on the point related to the security of networks there are several publicly available datasets such as KDD99, NSL- KDD, DARPA, and UNSWNB15 dataset. These datasets describe the behavior of networks through several identified network attributes known as features. The selection strategy for an optimal feature set improves the performance measures of the classifier and helps in the identification of the best features from the complete given dataset.

*Hence we have identified three methods of Feature Selection on a publicly available dataset and observed the performance of ML classifiers on each of the feature set in order to predict the best set of features from the dataset*.

### 9\. PROPOSED FEATURE SELECTION METHOD AND COMPARATIVE ANALYSIS OF MACHINE LEARNING ALGORITHMS

After discussing the point of security concerns and the applied methodologies that are mitigating these effects in wireless sensor networks, we describe the Feature Selection strategy and perform an evaluative analysis of machine learning algorithms on a publicly available dataset. The working of the proposed model has been depicted in Fig. ([**2**](#9781681088624-C2.html#f2)).

![](Images/9781681088624-C2_F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
Flow diagram of methodology. 

**Overview of the Adopted Method**

1.  A suitable dataset has been collected, and the stages of data cleaning and data preprocessing have been performed. Thus, a final clean dataset has been prepared for processing.
2.  Several feature selection strategies have been applied and the best feature set has been identified.
3.  Evaluative Analysis of the ML algorithms on each of the feature sets has been performed and a comparison has been drawn in terms of performance measures for the identification of the best feature set and algorithm.

### 9.1. Dataset Gathering

KDD99 dataset has been used for the evaluation procedure. The dataset comprises instances of several security attacks that have been grouped into four basic categories of security attacks. The KDD 99 dataset consists of twenty-two different attacks known as denial of service attack, User to root, remote to local and probing attacks.

### 9.2. Feature Selection Methodology

The feature selection is an important step of the data preprocessing stage, as it helps in the reduction of dimensionality as well as removes irrelevant features.

#### 9.2.1. Correlation-based Feature

The strategy comprises of two methods; firstly selection of compatible features from class and performing recognition and secondly elimination of redundant features from the dataset.

The correlation coefficient takes values +- 1 for the linearly dependent features and marks the value 0 if they are not related to each other.

#### 9.2.2. Info Gain Method

This is an evaluation method based upon the entropy and amount of information given by features for text. Entropy is important in decision tress as it decides the spitting of data and it affects how boundaries of decision trees are drawn using equation 3.

| ![](Images/9781681088624-C2_equ3.jpg) || (3) |
| --- | --- |

Consider a dataset (D) and a variable (v), the information gain is calculated as follows shown by equation 4;

| ![](Images/9781681088624-C2_equ4.jpg) || (4) |
| --- | --- |

Where IG (D, v) denotes information for dataset (D) for a variable (v), H(S) denotes entropy of the dataset before the change and H (D/V) denotes the conditional entropy for a dataset with a given variable (v).

#### 9.2.3. CFS Subset Evaluation

The worth of attributes is evaluated using each feature’s predictive ability and the degree of redundancy. Feature subsets that have interred correlation are preferred and these features are highly correlated.

### 10\. EXPERIMENTAL RESULTS AND ANALYSIS

After performing the data cleaning and removing duplicates from the dataset, the above feature selection strategies are applied. Machine Learning classifiers are then evaluated on all the three derived subsets, and performance measure for each classifier on each subset has been recorded.

*   Reduce features to 14 through Info Gain attribute selection

*   Step1: Read the dataset (D)

*   Step3: Return the weak classifiers
*   Step4: Set a normalization factor
*   Step5: Update weights and produce a strong classifier

## CONCLUSION AND FUTURE WORK

Hence we have evaluated three methodologies of feature selection on a publicly available dataset and observed that the feature set identified through Info Gain Method has provided better accuracy measures for the classifiers. The selection of features depends upon the type of dataset used, and the Random tree is the best machine algorithm for this method on the used dataset. The training and testing time for the feature set using Info gain has also been reduced.

Machine learning is safeguarding the wireless sensor networks against several sorts of denial of service security attacks. Researchers have mentioned that there is a need to develop a proactive resistance system for the detection of intrusions in wireless sensor networks. Wireless sensor networks are protected against these security breaches using frameworks based on machine learning that has provided efficient detection and accuracy. Machine learning designs a framework that provides a high level of security with reduced computational complexity. Future researchers can focus on designing optimal solutions against security attacks in wireless sensor networks and reducing the memory overheads.

Machine Learning is emerging as a promising technology for the future of wireless sensor networks as they are capable of handling issues related to capacity and design-related issues in sensor networks. The strategy adopted by ML algorithms, which train the model from previous experiences, made the sensor networks more optimized. ML algorithm's ability to work in a dynamic environment has made the wireless sensor networks more sophisticated. Future works should be focused on designing optimal solutions using machine learning algorithms for increasing the network’s lifetime, and models must also focus on clustering of nodes and mobility of sink nodes.
