## 1\. INTRODUCTION

“The blockchain revolution has a greater potential than anything we’ve seen in history. It’s bigger than the Internet revolution, how it’s going to restructure society.” —Patrick Byrne

The above remark made by Patrick Byrne, CEO and founder of Overstock.com, is as apt as it can be. Today, Blockchain technology is taking the globe by storm. The enormous amount of buzz and hype that this technology has created is attracting the attention of a massive number of researchers and scientists and will eventually create the dawn of a new revolution. This chapter aims to provide a brief but clear understanding of the important aspects of this technology among the readers.

The ultimate objective of mankind is the absolute modernization of his lifestyle for attaining the highest levels of comfort. This can practically be achieved only with the development and evolution of science and technology, which eventually gives us a plethora of immensely useful applications that make life easy for us. We are currently living in an era where the world is changing at a rapid pace, and there is a constant shift of the common mass towards contemporary technologies. These innovations have affected every industry, be it business, healthcare, energy, financial markets, and so on, to the extent that in recent times, these technologies have reached the comfort of our homes and affected the way we live and socialize \[[1](#9781681088624-C5.html#r1)\]. In this world powered by technology, everything comes at a cost We are living in times where we are experiencing a gradual transition from cash to cashless payments. Electronic Cash or E-Cash, the digital form of cash, is allowing easy and hassle-free transactions of money over the internet. This invention is in and of itself a commendable achievement that has caused a stir in the world of trade and commerce, and the immense importance of which can be understood during pandemics like Covid-19. But cybercrooks, with their felonious talents, are ever-ready to make some profit by breaching data of the transactions being carried out. Secure online transactions that involve third parties also cannot always be trusted. Let alone cybercriminals and private third parties, it has often been seen that the economic growth and development of most of the developing countries is often impeded by corrupt government officials who misuse their authority to satisfy their greed for monetary gains. Blockchain technology, which first came into the picture with the advent of Bitcoin in 2008 \[[2](#9781681088624-C5.html#r2)\], promises decentralized and secure transactions over the internet and is a successful attempt to solve these problems.

Blockchain is a magical technology that can be clubbed with almost all other technologies available today to add to their benefits and make them safe and secure for use. It is a Distributed Ledger Technology that records and maintains transactions chronologically, occurring on a peer-to-peer scale where every peer essentially maintains a copy of the transactions being carried out. An important point to be noted is that ‘no third party is involved’ and validation of new blocks to be added to the chain occurs only if at least 51% of the participants vote in favor, otherwise it is rejected. Now you must be wondering, what are blocks? They can simply be understood as pages of a record book that constantly register the latest transaction being carried out. Once a block runs out of memory (think of it as a page being filled with records), a new block is needed to be generated for which voting is done for its final validation and generation.

Blockchain is a revolutionary technology that came into practical existence with the advent of the cryptocurrency, Bitcoins, which caused a lot of excitement among the people and has intrigued researchers to study it thoroughly. Learning about it would help us remain updated with the most demanding skills of today. The true potential of this revolutionary technology needs to be understood by all so that we can make its best use. It is expected to re-shape the industrial trends and cause a transition from paper-based/non-secure online transactions to secure, privacy-preserving, immutable e-transactions.

This chapter will give you a basic idea of Blockchain Technology. Each topic is explained in a detailed but crisp manner. The authors have tried to make the chapter more interesting by incorporating a hands-on activity about the deployment of smart contracts to attract the interest in this topic of the readers. It is hoped to generate a clear understanding among the readers about this incredible technology which is way ahead of its time.

In the following chapter, we will first study how the need for this technology arose, followed by its history. We will then be introduced to the different elements that are essentially a part of every blockchain network. We will then learn about its features owing to which this technology has become immensely important in today’s world. Next, we will study the types, working, applications, advantages, and challenges related to this topic.

 

## 2\. THE NEED FOR BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY

The need for this technology arose with the International Banking Crisis which led to the collapse of investment in 2008. Bitcoin cryptocurrency, which was the first practical example of this technology, brings along with it many advantages such as efficiency, cost-effectiveness, reliability, and a privacy-preserving secure system for creating and recording financial transactions. With an ever-increasing human population and urbanization, the number of transactions being carried out is increasing exponentially and is speculated to affect the complexity, efficiency, and cost of the current systems and in turn, increase its vulnerability \[[3](#9781681088624-C5.html#r3)\]. The current digital transactions rely on trusted third parties to fulfill their objectives. Whether these parties can be trusted or not is a big question. Though these third parties which can be anyone- a Government Agency or a private owner- claim that the transactions are carried out in a secure environment, is it so? Studies have shown that even though a few central authorities follow strict protocols to preserve the privacy and security of transactions to the extent that they provide certificates of assurance, these technologies are not foolproof. For example, SSL certificates that ensure us that we are on a secure website have also been stolen from few very well-known domains of Microsoft, Twitter, Facebook, and Central Intelligence Agency (CIA), UK.

Also, in today’s world where everything is done at the click of a mouse, we need a technology that is efficient, fast, and at the same time affordable. Blockchain is the perfect solution to this. People don’t need to pay money to any third party, like a bank or a private organization, to maintain their records as digital ledgers which can be monitored with ease and precision owing to the peer-to-peer, distributed, and decentralized nature of the Blockchain architecture. The world needs a technology like Blockchain which provides a safe and secure platform for carrying out the transactions, complete ownership and authority over one’s property, and also transparent transactions to be carried out. The data once stored on the network cannot be changed or deleted because of its immutability. This is an immensely important feature that can be used to store extremely important and private data on the system. This technology also addresses another very important issue- the problem of Double-Spending. Since the records are maintained strictly chronologically, one can easily keep a track of the money spent and avoid the problem and loss caused by double-spending \[[4](#9781681088624-C5.html#r4)\]. Having understood its need, let us now learn about this technology in detail.

 

## 3\. HISTORY AND EVOLUTION

This topic first came into existence on 31^(st) October 2008, when Satoshi Nakamoto’s white paper “Bitcoin: A Peer-to-Peer Electronic Cash System” was released \[[2](#9781681088624-C5.html#r2)\]. The paper discussed a “peer-to-peer version of electronic cash”, which allowed online payments to be sent directly from one party to the other without involving a bank or any other financial institution. This discussion is incomplete without mentioning Bitcoins. Bitcoin is the first practical example of Blockchain technology, that came into existence in 2009 and is the first-ever cryptocurrency to be developed and is the most popular example of this technology. It is an innovative method of payment that has introduced virtual cash and is one of its kind. Considering its design, it only allows the exchange and distribution of digital information and bars its modification. You must remember that Blockchain and Bitcoin are not the same things. Blockchain is the technology used for the creation of the digital currency, Bitcoin. Let us understand this with the help of an example. A mobile phone is just a small metallic object without an Operating System (OS). OS adds life to the phone and is simply software that manages the tasks and its worth can only be understood by the applications it can host. Considering this example, a Blockchain is analogous to the Operating System and Bitcoins to the applications it holds.

To date, three generations of Blockchain have emerged and the fourth one is under evolution. Let’s quickly discuss the generations in brief:

### 3.1. First Generation/ Blockchain 1.0

They were simple ledgers that recorded transactions and were built to improve the existing monetary system. Examples of first-generation cryptocurrencies include Bitcoin, Litecoin, *etc*. All these cryptocurrencies are written using C++ and consider the Bitcoin source code as their basis. Also, they use the Proof-of-Work Mechanism which makes these networks pretty costly because the mining involved here requires a lot of electricity.

### 3.2. Second Generation/ Blockchain 2.0

It emerged in 2015 with the advent of Ethereum which has much wider functionalities. The most important feature that was added in this generation is the Smart Contracts that make the transactions secure by allowing the automatic execution of all conditions. It is written using Solidity programming language and suffers from the problem of scalability.

### 3.3. Third Generation/ Blockchain 3.0

It comes with more advantages, like better design and wider functionality, and has tried to address the issue of scalability. Some examples are Cardano, Nano, *etc*. Instead of Proof-of-Work, it incorporates the Proof-Of-Stake consensus mechanism.

### 3.4. Fourth Generation/ Blockchain 4.0

It has yet to come into existence but it is expected that this generation will evolve when Artificial Intelligence will meet this technology. Keeping in mind the fast pace of research and the growing interest of scientists, it seems that that day is not far when Blockchain 4.0 will become a reality.

 

## 4\. PREFATORY ELEMENTS

This section introduces you to the generic elements of Blockchain technology.

 

### 4.1. Transaction

A transaction can be defined as an agreement between a supplier and a consumer that there will be an exchange of assets either in the present or in the future. It is the fundamental unit of Blockchain and represents exchange by transfer of values from one participant to another \[[5](#9781681088624-C5.html#r5)\]. A transaction includes the recipient, signature identifying the sender, amount of cryptocurrency being used for the transaction to be brought into action, gas price (cost paid by the sender per computational step), and the gas limit (maximum number of steps a transaction is supposed to be executed in). In technical terms, the transaction can be defined as the product of gas price and the amount of gas used.

 

### 4.2. Node

The term node refers to the user or participant who is a part of the Blockchain platform. They form the building blocks of the Blockchain Infrastructure. When an attempt to add a new block to the chain is made, it is reflected on all the nodes in the system, and depending upon how authentic a transaction is, the nodes can together accept or reject the proposal. Nodes can either be online or offline. Online nodes are active participants in the network while offline modes need to catch up with the latest advances by downloading the new blocks that were added when they were in offline conditions. The process of updating the offline blocks is known as synchronization.

 

### 4.3. Mining

It is the process of adding transactions to the distributed public ledger technology, blockchain, and it is this process that facilitates the safe exchange of assets in the network. Miners are the nodes that are capable of mining. They have to solve a cryptographic puzzle for the creation of valid blocks and the miner who solves the puzzle first gets to add a block to the networked chain. They broadcast their block on the network which then requires verification from all the nodes in the network irrespective of whether they are simple nodes or miners. If the block is voted to be valid following the consensus mechanisms (which is explained in Section 4.7 of this Chapter), the block is added to the chain, otherwise, it is rejected.

 

### 4.4. Block

A ‘block’ can simply be defined as a collection of transactions that are bundled together logically. It is these blocks that are chained together to form Blockchain technology.

Structurally, a block consists of a block header and a block body \[[6](#9781681088624-C5.html#r6)\]. The block header includes the Block Version, Merkle Tree Root Hash, Hash of parent block, Time Stamp, nBits, Nonce (Number used only once), and the Block Body includes all the transactions carried out in that block. Block Header includes the block metadata which can be visualized in Fig. (**[1](#9781681088624-C5.html#f1)**) and better understood from the list that follows:

![](Images/9781681088624-C5_F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Structure of a Block. 

*   Block version – It refers to the current version of the block structure.
*   Merkle Tree Root Hash - A cryptographic hash of all of the transactions that a block contains.
*   Hash of parent block – The hash value of the preceding/parent block, which is always linked to the succeeding/child block.
*   Time Stamp - The exact time at which a block was created.
*   nBits – It refers to the target, *i.e*., the threshold value below which a block header hash must be for a block to be valid.
*   Nonce (“number used once”) - A random value that is added to a hashed block which, when re-hashed, matched the difficult restrictions.

Each block in the Blockchain is linked to two other blocks- one, its parent block, and two, its child block. It is to be remembered that there can only be one parent to a child block and a single child to a parent block as shown in Fig. (**[2](#9781681088624-C5.html#f2)**).

![](Images/9781681088624-C5_F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
How different blocks are linked together. 

The first block to be added to a Blockchain network is known as “Genesis Block”.

**Types of Blocks:**

 

*   Main branch Blocks- They extend the longest chain in the network, *i.e*., the main blockchain.
*   Side Branch Blocks- Though they are currently not present practically, they are expected to be incorporated as a part of the main branch in a side chain soon, due to the growth of transaction rate.
*   Orphan Blocks- Those blocks that point to a parent block that is unknown to the node processing it.

### 4.5. Hashing

Hashing is the process by which input of simple integers and strings is converted into a complicated, non-decipherable encrypted output using complex mathematical algorithms. Hashes of any length of input always give a fixed length of output and this makes it practically impossible to guess the original word. It is a ‘one-way function’ and can not be cracked. So how does it help in checking the authenticity of a message? Let’s understand this with the help of an example. Suppose you want to download some important official document that is highly confidential. For this purpose, you will need a hash of the document and the digital signature of the sender which in turn is also hashed. The document message is encrypted and made it impossible to decipher using hashing algorithms. They act as a trap-door function that prevents it from being traced. The receiver decrypts the message using the same hashing function and signature and compares the two hash values. If the same hash value is produced, it can be said that the document and signature are both authentic. There are many algorithms available for this purpose, like different versions of the Secure Hashing Algorithm (SHA) – SHA1, SHA 224, SHA256, SHA512, *etc*., Message Digest Algorithm (MD) – MD2, MD4, MD5, Keccak – Keccak-256, Keccak 512, *etc*., Shake(Secure Hash Algorithm and KECCAK) with versions 128 and 256. Shake is different in the sense that it produces any length of the output.

Let us see how each of these algorithms produces different outputs for the same word. Suppose the word is “Abracadabra”, the following Table **[1](#9781681088624-C5.html#t1)** shows its corresponding hash value using a few of the many different algorithms available.

Table 1 **Showing the different hash values produced using different hashing algorithms for the same message.**  

| **Algorithm Used** || **Message hash for “Abracadabra”** |
| --- | --- |
| *SHA256* || 46abe7c8d7f5033272383a4a2483ca72ca35c6bf7d8b4b9d60eee94fd7aebce2 |
| *SHA512* || e31635ff8390c8df27ea5ecd810a73c92ba62a399fafe52b78467abf21d9e4adaf833  
31238905f491bdcc1d7bfddf4d89074b4599638d4504c4eedfde45c62a9 |
| *MD5* || 53a5e9533a3a2dd2685f4f112b82379a |
| *Keccak-256* || cdbeda58b615b3bbdc510ac7b6aa14e618709a6c994e062e5e34e37e5992ab7a |
| *Shake-256* || abb7c5ab0ddcc178933e95cd40466fd2f6f7190ad1bdcb72e59e8fe447972f94da72  
7056749cc535d94fead6a0ba697184427fd1fcf31ed5ef65e927ef943d00 |

  

The hash value for two messages will always be different. Therefore, hashing safeguards the privacy of the participants by ensuring secure transmission of the message intended for a particular recipient only.

 

### 4.6. Digital Signatures

The data to be transacted has to be secured using digital signatures which ensure that the data is not meddled with. The purpose of using digital signatures is to bind the signatory to the message which is to be sent. For this purpose, the participants use cryptography techniques which may either be public-key cryptography or private key cryptography. Digital signatures are the digital counterpart of handwritten signatures and should ideally be non-forgeable by any attacker. Let us gain an understanding of how digital signatures work. To sign a document, the sender uses two mutually authenticating keys, that is, the sender’s private key and the receiver’s public key. The mathematical algorithm used acts like a cipher, by encrypting the data and creating a hash of the message. Only the correct receiver can decode the document by using his private key and the sender’s public key. If in any case the document changes after signing, the digital signature becomes invalid. There are several digital signature algorithms available.

Following Fig. (**[3](#9781681088624-C5.html#f3)**) shows a flowchart of secure data transmission in the Blockchain platform.

 

### 4.7. Consensus

It is an important part of the blockchain architecture and is used for the verification and validation of transactions and blocks in the Blockchain is known as Consensus. It follows a set of self-enforced rules which every participant in the network has to abide by and thus helps in maintaining the integrity and synchronization of the nodes that are a part of this chain \[[7](#9781681088624-C5.html#r7)\]. It also ensures that an identical and updated set of copies of the transactions is distributed among the participants. Its advantage is that it provides security, privacy, and transparency in the network. Some consensus algorithms are mentioned below:

*   Proof-of-Work (POW)- The core idea behind this algorithm is to solve a complex mathematical puzzle that easily gives out a solution. The mathematical problem, in reality, uses a lot of computational power. The solution to the problem should be a number less than the hash of the block and the miner who solves the puzzle first is given some reward in terms of cryptocurrency. Ethereum, Litecoin, Dogecoin are few cryptocurrencies that use this algorithm.
*   Proof-of-Stake (POS)- The essence of this algorithm is the same as a POW, but the approach is different. Here, there are ‘validators’ instead of miners who bet on the blocks they think will be added to the chain. Based on which block is added to the chair, their reward is proportionate to their investment and their stakes increase in value.
*   Delegated Proof of Stake (DPoS)- The main idea behind this is that participants stake a few of their coins and elect delegates on their behalf. The weightage given to each delegate depends upon the investment made. It is thus one of the fastest consensus algorithms.
*   Practical Byzantine Fault Tolerance (PBFT)- The basic idea behind this algorithm is that, depending upon the status of the ledger, the algorithm works even in the presence of a few faulty nodes or when a few nodes fail to respond. Hyperledger Fabric, Tendermint, Zilliqa are few platforms that use this algorithm \[[8](#9781681088624-C5.html#r8), [9](#9781681088624-C5.html#r9)\].
*   Proof of Activity (PoA)- This is an energy-efficient and fast algorithm which uses a hybrid approach by clubbing the PoW and PoS algorithms. Here, validators or verified accounts are entrusted with the task of ensuring the correctness of the block.
*   Proof of Capacity (PoC)- It is also called Proof of Space and is similar to the Proof of Work algorithm, where the computation of PoW is analogous to storage in PoC. The solution to the complex mathematical problems is accumulated in the storage used to produce blocks and the fastest one in computing the solution stands a better chance of creating blocks. The process which this algorithm follows is known as ‘plotting’.
*   Proof of Burn (PoB)- In this algorithm, instead of taking money, the participants burn their coins/ cryptocurrency by sending it to an address from where it is irretrievable. The greater the amount of cryptocurrency they burn, the greater is their chance of being selected for the next block.
*   Proof of Elapsed Time (PoET)- It works by generating a random sleep time for each participant during which the particular nodes go into sleep mode. If a block is available when a particular node wakes up or becomes active, that participant is considered the lucky winner and gets a chance to commit a new block to the Blockchain.

![](Images/9781681088624-C5_F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
How does Blockchain work?

| **Consensus Algorithm** || **Node Identity Management** || **Energy saving** || **Adversary tolerance power** || **Blockchain Type** || **Transaction Rate** || **Example** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PoW || Open || High || <25% of Computing Power || Permissionless || Low || Bitcoin, Litecoin, Ethereum  
(till 2018) |
| PoS || Open || Medium || <51% of Stake || Both || High || Ethereum (since 2018) |
| DPoS || Open || Low || <51% of Validators || Both || Medium || Lisk, Bitshares, Waykichain, Steem |
| PBFT || Permissioned || High || <33.3% of Faulty Replicas || Permissioned || High || Hyperledger Fabric |
| PoA || Open || High || <25% of Computing Power || Permissioned || Medium || Microsoft Azure, Decred |
| PoC || Open || Medium || <25% of Computing Power || Permissionless || Medium || SpaceMint, BurstCoin, Chia |
| Tendermint || Permissioned || High || <33.3& of Byzantine Voting Power || Permissioned || High || Tendermint |
| Ripple || Open || High || <20% of Faulty Nodes || Both || High || Ripple |
| PoB || Open || High || <51% of Stake || Permissionless || Medium || SlimCoin |
| PoET || Open || Low || <25% of Computing Power || Both || Medium || Hyperledger Sawtooth |

  

### 4.8. Smart Contract

A contract, as we all know, is a legal bond between two parties. A smart contract refers to a piece of code that is stored on the Blockchain network and executed automatically when certain conditions are met. Colloquially, they are self-executing scripts encoding set protocols in Blockchain that digitally verify and implement the contract made between participants and ultimately help in transferring digital assets \[[10](#9781681088624-C5.html#r10)\]. Smart contracts were first introduced by Ethereum and are written using the Solidity programming language. There are many tools available for authoring Smart Contracts, but the web-based tool Remix is the handiest among all.

Let’s quickly perform a fun activity by creating and deploying a smart contract on an Ethereum test network. For this, we will first download the browser extension of MetaMask. MetaMask is a browser plugin that serves as an Ethereum Browser as well as a wallet. Once downloaded, we need to create a wallet. We can use any one of the test Networks available to get some Dummy Ethers in our wallet because the deployment of a smart contract cannot be done for free. (Ether is the cryptocurrency of Ethereum). For this, we need to select one of the test networks available as shown in Fig. (**[4](#9781681088624-C5.html#f4)**) (I have used the Goerli Test Network because it is comparatively easier to get test Ethers on this network, but you can choose any) and open the Testnet Faucet browser of that network. By providing our account address, we can request them to give us test Ethers.

Having done this, we will open the remix.ethereum.org webpage and create our contract using the solidity language. Let us build a smart contract for the electricity bill. For this, we will create a new file with the ‘.sol’ extension and follow the steps shown in Figs. (**[5](#9781681088624-C5.html#f5)** and **[6](#9781681088624-C5.html#f6)**).

## 5\. FEATURES

### 5.1. Distributed

It is a distributed technology that allows data/transactions to be synchronized and shared among all entities in the chain instead of being copied or transferred. What needs to be understood here is that due to the absence of a single data storehouse and transactions being distributed among all the entities, the records/ money can never be hacked or stolen. This also prevents load on a single server by distributing the computational power among the many computers on the network. Advantages:

 

*   Better and more efficient outcome due to distributed computational power
*   Ownership to all members is assured because they actively participate in all the transactions.
*   Since the replica of each transaction gets stored on all the nodes, tampering with the data is impossible because any fraudulent activity can easily be detected.

![](Images/9781681088624-C5_F6.jpg)**Fig. (6))**  
Transaction details in the Etherscan Network. 

### 5.2. Decentralized

It means that there is no need for a governing authority or third party to manage the data and transactions. This implies that it is an open ledger technology and ensures full integrity among all the members who are a part of the blockchain because every entity in the chain has the same copy of the ledger. By this feature, the users remain super active, alert, and always updated regarding all the transactions that take place \[[11](#9781681088624-C5.html#r11)\]. To list a few advantages of these features:

 

*   No need for third parties to drastically increases the speed of the transaction process
*   Data will never be lost because each user has a copy of it
*   Lower transaction cost
*   No possibility of scams

Note: The two terms mentioned above might seem similar in context but in reality, they are very different. Decentralization relates to the level of control and the authority they share in decision-making whereas distributed network refers to the difference in location of the users. In a decentralized network, the decision is made based on the voting done by all the nodes. As stated earlier, if at least 51% of the participants vote in favor of something, that action is performed, or else it is rejected. But in a distributed system, the processing is shared among all the participants, but it might allow the decision to be made exclusively by a central authority. Blockchain incorporates both these features.

### 5.3. Immutable

Data once added to the Blocks can never be changed because it gets permanently stored on the Blockchain network forever. This means that data that was first added to the Bitcoin network when it was created is still safe and secure and can easily be accessed. What makes it different from Databases regarding this feature is that Databases allow us to Update and Delete data stored on their network, in addition to the creation and addition of data. However, this is not the case in Blockchain. While it allows us to Create and Add data, Updating and Deletion is not possible. It adds to its security because transaction records since Day 1 are stored just as they are and any attempt to tamper with the data goes in vain. Both the sender and receiver can rest assured that their data will never be meddled with.

### 5.4. Transparent

The addition of a single block or transaction between any two persons is reflected on all the blocks. Thus, nothing is hidden from anybody and changes are viewable publicly. It is one advantageous feature. Let’s understand how. Imagine that a fraudulent participant has gained illegal access to the network and is pretending to be some other person. He can just not make any transaction because firstly, it needs to be validated by all, and secondly and most importantly, the private key is a unique key which is almost impossible to guess correctly, and in case a person tries a few keys, all these attempts are reflected on all the blocks and he can easily be traced.

### 5.5. Peer to Peer Network

Blockchain allows transactions to occur securely between two parties excluding the need of any third party. Each transaction is signed digitally by using some sort of Cryptographic Algorithm. This lets only the true receiver decipher the code with the knowledge of the sender’s public key and his private key. Hashing algorithms used for signature generation are of immense importance because they act like a trapdoor and make them practically impossible for a cybercriminal to crack it.

 

## 6\. TYPES AND PLATFORMS

This technology can be divided into 3 types \[[12](#9781681088624-C5.html#r12)\]:

### 6.1. Public Blockchain

As the name suggests, this type is open to use by the general public and is not owned by any single authority. Anyone can participate in the consensus method of decision-making. All participants are given equal authority to decide the fate of the ledger by following the 51% rule, according to which if 51% of the participants vote in favor of a decision, for example, creation of a block, it is created, otherwise, the proposal is discarded. Each user actively participates in the transaction verification process. The users may or might not be rewarded for their involvement. To understand this clearly, imagine that you are a part of a public Blockchain network. A request for someone’s new transaction pops up on your screen. To verify it, you are supposed to solve some mathematical problems and if you are the first one to give the correct answer, you are rewarded some money in terms of online Blockchain-based currency. The answers given by the other participants are equally important for decision-making, but they are not always rewarded. This type of Blockchain can be used to conduct votes during elections or in maintaining cryptocurrencies.

### 6.2. Private Blockchain

Private Blockchain, just like its name, are private. The accessibility and transactions are restricted to the members of the chain. Members may include organizations or some individuals who share personal data among themselves. The authority on data management is strictly monitored and controlled. This type of Blockchain might be used for some private bank, or a particular company to maintain their business records or a university to maintain the records of its students, staff, official documents, *etc*. It cannot be accessed by the general public. Platforms include R3 Corda, Ripple

### 6.3. Consortium Blockchain

It is also known as a semi-private or hybrid blockchain and incorporates both public and private blockchain networks. It can be perceived as a semi-decentralized network. It can be hosted on a private network where a few nodes can form a public blockchain just to incorporate its best features of stability, security, and verification. This Blockchain might be used for storing data of a mobile company for example, for which read-only permission is granted to the public but read and write access is provided to a few selected employees. Some notable platforms are Hyperledger, MultiChain, *etc*.

Following Table **[3](#9781681088624-C5.html#t3)** briefly compares the three types of Blockchain:

Table 3 **Comparison of the three types of blockchain.**  

| **Property** || **Public Blockchain** || **Private Blockchain** || **Consortium Blockchain** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| *Access* || Open || Limited to members || Partly open |
| *Transparency* || Completely transparent || Transparent only to users || Depends upon set rules |
| *Decentralization* || Yes || No || Partly |
| *Reward* || Participants rewarded for growing the chain || No incentives known || Might or might not reward participants (depends on set rules) |
| *Speed* || Comparatively slow || Faster || Somewhere in between |
| *Efficiency* || Low || High || High |
| *Consensus Determination* || All miners || Selected nodes || One authority |
| *Use case* || Almost every industry like cryptocurrency, supply-chain management, stock market, etc. || For organizations who need to keep their data private or carry out personal tasks || For organizations who want to make the best use of public blockchain features but want to keep their information completely private. |
| *Platform* || Bitcoin, Ethereum || Corda, Ripple || Hyperledger |

  
 

## 7\. HOW DOES IT WORK?

Having studied the basic elements of a Blockchain, we now know that all records in the ledger are stored in blocks that are secured by digital signatures made using cryptographic functions. Blocks are intangible and immutable. New blocks are created only when the old one runs out of memory. In a public Blockchain, when a request for new data to be added to the blockchain is received, the request is reflected on all the nodes in the network. If the transaction is approved following the consensus rules, it is validated by adding a timestamp and added to the blockchain network by linking it to the previous block. This decision as to which nodes will append the block is very critical because cybercriminals might be ready and lucky enough to cause the 51% attack. For this reason, the mechanism of the consensus algorithm being used also becomes extremely important. A transaction is made secure following the cryptographic algorithms. The main steps of how a Blockchain transaction is approved/ carried out are listed below:

 

*   Step 1: Transaction request is reflected on all nodes in the network.
*   Step 2: Consensus mechanisms are followed for its approval/ rejection.
*   Step 3: Transaction is approved and validated.
*   Step 4: Block gets added to the chain and the transaction is carried out.

 

## 8\. APPLICATIONS, ADVANTAGES & CHALLENGES

 

### 8.1. APPLICATIONS

With its features and ability to carry out transactions within a short period, Blockchain is one such technology that can blend with almost all the technologies out there \[[13](#9781681088624-C5.html#r13)\]. It is slowly impacting many different sectors, thereby making their task more efficient and secure. A few applications are listed below:

 

1.  Blockchain-based Cryptocurrency- The most well-known example being Bitcoin, other cryptocurrencies like Litecoin and Ethers are also being developed. This can be attributed to the distributed, decentralization, immutability, and transparency features of this technology. DCEP by the Central Bank of China was launched on 29th August 2020 to develop an electronic cash infrastructure by providing anonymous digital wallets with no bank account \[[14](#9781681088624-C5.html#r14)\].
2.  Secure sharing of Medical data- Healthcare is one sector where reliable activity tracking is needed and records need to be maintained perfectly. Introducing Blockchain technology in this field will make the best use of its potential and drastically improve healthcare facilities \[[15](#9781681088624-C5.html#r15)^(\-)[18](#9781681088624-C5.html#r18)\].
3.  In Supply Chain Management- Here Blockchain is needed to provide secure and complete information and to maintain proper integrity between the members, who need to be updated regularly about the products being developed \[[19](#9781681088624-C5.html#r19)\].
4.  In Business Applications- It helps save time and money and ensures proper management and safe monitoring of each step of progress \[[20](#9781681088624-C5.html#r20)\].
5.  In Elections- In democratic countries where elections take place to elect their leaders, Blockchain can be used to carry out fair elections where all records are maintained easily and the personal identity of the voters is not revealed \[[21](#9781681088624-C5.html#r21)\].
6.  Government-based application for storing personal documents- Governments can use Blockchain platforms to store and exchange the personal documents of the people. Safe storage of these documents, especially in places that are prone to annual calamities like floods, is immensely helpful because the document is stored safely forever and can never be tampered with.

 

### 8.2. ADVANTAGES

The features of Blockchain technology already discussed in section 5 of this chapter can also be taken as its advantages. A few advantages have already been mentioned there. However, if you sit and think about it, you will realize that there are many more advantages to this technology that is reshaping the financial world. A few other advantages are mentioned below:

 

1.  The biggest advantage is that it addresses the privacy and security concerns of the users by using cryptographic hash functions.
2.  Authentic transactions: Data once entered cannot be deleted or tampered with and also every block contains a permanent time-stamp of the transactions. This makes the whole process as authentic as any other and adds to the security and privacy features.
3.  Faster speed and Increased efficiency: Because the entire system is decentralized and distributed and not dependent on some central authority for transactions or data storage, the speed of the business being carried out drastically increases and the efficiency improves. This also means that time taken for one transaction reduces which is a major advantage because of time constraints in recent times.
4.  Easy to use: We got a brief exposure to the deployment of smart contracts in the network and saw how easy it is to work on this platform. We can safely carry out our transactions from anywhere and anytime, unlike financial organizations that have time restrictions.
5.  Improved records and Better Traceability: As each block contains metadata including the Block version, timestamp, Merkle Tree Root Hash, Hash of parent block, bits, and Nonce (Number used only once), it becomes very easy and hassle-free to trace them even if they were generated 10 years ago. The fact that Blocks created since day 1 include a timestamp and are all intact and can never be deleted or meddled with indicates that the records are all maintained in chronological order. This advantage can, among others, best be put to use in the healthcare sector where a proper record of a patient’s medical history needs to be stored.
6.  Since transactions are added to a block only after the process of maximum trust verification by using consensus protocols to validate the entry, it eliminates risks like double-entry which can cause double payments to occur.

### 8.3. CHALLENGES

There is still a lot of excitement in the tech world about Blockchain Technology, and the excitement is only increasing every day. This technology is no doubt the most influential and thrilling in the 21^(st) century, and we have already gone through quite a few applications and advantages of this technology. However, this technology is still in its developmental phase because of the few challenges it brings along with it, some of which are mentioned below:

 

1.  Scalability- This is one of the biggest challenges, especially in public blockchains, which need to be resolved soon. Storage requirement has become an issue. Let us understand this with the help of the Bitcoin example. In Bitcoin, the size of one block is restricted to 1 MB and in general, a block is mined almost every 10 min. Also, a Bitcoin network has a limitation of 7 transactions/second, which is not capable of dealing with high-frequency trade. Also, larger blocks require more storage space and a slower rate of propagation \[[22](#9781681088624-C5.html#r22)\]. This has the possibility of causing a shift to centralized networks that can maintain such a huge chain of transactions. This is causing a trade-off between security and block size, which is a major concern \[[23](#9781681088624-C5.html#r23), [24](#9781681088624-C5.html#r24)\].
2.  Limited Supply to increase demand- The total number of Bitcoins which will ever be available is 21 million. It has been predicted that the last of them will be mined in approximately 2140, and we have already mined 17 million bitcoins to date.
3.  Energy Consumption- This technology uses the Proof-of-Work mechanism for proper validation of the transactions and the ultimate addition to the network. This process uses a lot of computational power to reflect transactions on every node, solve complex mathematical problems required for the processing, and verification of transactions \[[25](#9781681088624-C5.html#r25)\].
4.  Interoperability- Most Blockchain networks work in silos and are incapable of exchanging information with other Blockchain networks that are not a part of them. It can be speculated as one of the major reasons why most organizations are still not making a move towards this technology. Though attempts have been made towards resolving this issue, it has not helped much and a major portion of this problem still needs to be fixed.
5.  Lack of trained professionals is yet another challenge, but that is gradually being fixed with increasing awareness regarding this topic.

## 9\. FUTURE SCOPE

Though the pioneer implementer of this technology was the financial sector, it is expected to be adopted gradually by many other sectors. Some challenges need to be resolved, and it is expected to pave the way for new opportunities for scientists and researchers who work in this field. It is anticipated that the integration of this technology with other technologies like the Internet of Things, Artificial Intelligence, and Machine Learning will bring about incredible changes across the globe. Also, there is no standardization in terms of the Blockchain mechanisms followed and there are hundreds of different Blockchain platforms out there. Therefore, if a company wants to incorporate this technology in their workspace, they first need to find out which platform best suits their requirement. They can be done by Blockchain Testing wherein different parameters with special emphasis on the function to be performed is tested to check if the platform works as claimed by the developers \[[26](#9781681088624-C5.html#r26), [27](#9781681088624-C5.html#r27)\].

## CONCLUSION

Blockchain technology can be perceived as a distributed decentralized ledger technology that is transparent and immutable and stores records as transactions in time-stamped blocks. It allows the users to make and verify transactions fast without the need for any central authority. Though this technology is still in its infancy and its first applications, starting with the development of Bitcoin, and limited to the financial sector, it is slowly creeping into all other sectors and has the potential to transform the traditional industries with its features. When clubbed with other technologies like the Internet of Things, Artificial Intelligence, and Machine Learning, it can bring about spectacularly impressive changes that add to the modernization of human lives. Having developed a fundamental idea about what Blockchain is and also its applications, advantages, challenges, and future trends, we can conclude the chapter with the famous actor Fred Ehrsam’s remark, “While it may seem scarier in the earlier days, I think ultimately the blockchain creates a safer world.”
