### 2.1. IoT And Its Integration

Internet of Things promotes connectivity between humans and devices in our day-to-day life and enables a wide range of customized services to the end-users who might suffer from a lack of security. This issue can be overridden by the integration of blockchain technology. Each and every communication made by device 1 with other devices will generate a transaction. These transactions between the service provider and the acceptor will be stored in the blockchain which maintains a public digital ledger \[[8](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r8)\]. Blockchain in collaboration with IoT has achieved many targets like energy consumption, data aggregation, scalability, security and privacy, access control, authentication, non-repudiation, and so on with a great increase in efficiency and productivity \[[9](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r9)\].

 

## 3\. TECHNIQUES USED FOR IMPOSING SECURITY

As we already know, Blockchain is a distributed and decentralized network, which furnishes privacy and security along with transparency and immutability. From its decentralized nature, it is clear that there is no central authority to validate or verify the transactions. Even then blockchain assures its verification and security with the consensus protocol. To maintain its reliability, we can use distributed digital ledger along with cryptographic techniques.

 

### 3.1. Consensus

It is a common agreement that has to be satisfied by all the connected nodes of the blockchain network in a distributed ledger \[[10](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r10)\]. It has been classified as follows.

*   **PoW** – **Proof of Work** algorithm is used to confirm the transactions and provides a new block to the chain. It brings out the concept of mining. The person who does mining is known as a miner. Mining is the process of validating the transactions and maintaining them on a global ledger called a blockchain.
*   **PoS – Proof of Stake** has been created as an alternative for PoW. Here, the available balance of bitcoin will be considered instead of considering the resources to solve the puzzles. For example, a miner who holds only 4% of bitcoin in his account can only mine 4% of the blocks.
*   **PBFT – Practical Byzantine Fault Tolerance** occurs in a decentralized, distributed system like blockchain technology. There is a possibility for the participants to communicate with each other in an uncontrolled, open, and permission less system. There is a chance for an assumption that the transaction could be malicious if it does not reach the destination in the mentioned span of time. It can be overridden by having at least 2/3 of the most reliable network nodes. If the majority of the system decides to act maliciously, then it reaches 51% attack.

![](Images/9781681088624-C7_F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Byzantine Generals Problem. 

From the given Fig. ([**1**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f1)), we can notice that the dishonest delegate modifies the original message and sends it to other delegates. The problem occurs if other delegates trust and act according to the information shared by the dishonest one. To avoid such cases, blockchain implements certain consensus like PoW, PoS, and so on in order to select the most reliable network nodes. Here, every transaction of a single node will be automatically reflected in all other nodes. Therefore, there is no chance for modification.

 

*   **PoB**–Proof of Burn is fondly referred to as Proof of Work with less energy consumption. It does not require huge computational resources. Miners have to burn a certain amount of their own crypto-currencies like native coin or bitcoin to determine their commitment towards the network to gain the virtual mining, power (it was compared to virtual mining rig) to mine and validate the transactions. Similar to PoW, PoB also provides rewards to the miners for their effort in a specified period of time. Most of these rewards will cover the initial investment of the burned coins.
*   **PoC** – PoC stands for Proof of Capacity. The name itself clearly depicts that it is highly concerned with the capacity of the node. Greater the hard drive capacity, the greater the chance to become a miner or validator of the upcoming block.
*   **DAG**–Directed Acyclic Graph is a finite directed graph with no cycles. In order to promote blockchain technology in a more practical way, DAG has been suggested. It holds a series of nodes in a topological order connected together in the ledger. This newly proposed structure in the blockchain is termed the tangle. Each transaction must be validated by two former transactions \[[11](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r11)\].
*   **Pluggable Consensus –**The ordering of transactions is consigned to a modular component for consensus which will be logically decoupled from peers that execute transactions and maintains a ledger. Its platform relies on Byzantine Fault Tolerant (BFT) ordering \[[12](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r12)\].

Table ([**2**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7t2)) clearly describes the functionalities with the merits and demerits of consensus protocols in blockchain technology \[[13](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r13)\].

Table 2 **Comparison between the consensus protocols.**  

| **Protocol** || **Function** || **Language** || **Resource utilized** || **Processing Speed** || **Efficiency** || **Drawbacks** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PoW || Used to assure the transactions and build new blocks to the chain. || Solidity, Python, Java, *etc.*, || Elevated || Slow || Stunted || Higher the power consumption, lowers the security |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PoS || Available balance of bitcoin plays a major role || Native || Stunted || Fast || Elevated || Only wealthy stakeholders can enjoy the consensus control |
| PBFT || Voting mechanism will be considered to assure the integrity and origin of the transaction || Java and Go lang || Elevated || Elevated || Elevated || Excessive communication overhead |
| PoB || PoW with less energy consumption. It involves coin burning strategy. || Solidity, Go lang,  
C++ || Moderate || Moderate || Stunted || Wastage of resources |
| PoC || Increased hard drive capacity increases the chance of being a miner || \- || Elevated || Stunted || Elevated || Only nodes with greater capacity receive more stake |
| PoET || Each node produces a random number to evaluate its waiting time || Python || Elevated || Medium || Elevated || Chances repeatedly fall for the same node to be the leader |
| DAG || It has no cycles. Every node must be validated with 2 former nodes || \- || Stunted || Elevated || Elevated || Must be in a topological order |

  

*   **PoET** – In Proof of Elapsed Time, each node participating in the network is required to wait for a specified period of time. The first node which completes the designated waiting time wins the mining authority for a new block. Each node in the network generates an arbitrary waiting time and sleeps for that specified duration. The node which wakes up first will grab the chance for mining the block. The same process will be repeated for all the upcoming blocks.

 

### 3.2. Ledger

In general, a ledger is termed as a record-keeping system where we can maintain all the financial transactions (credit or debit) of a person or a business for future reference. It can be differentiated into two types.

Centralized ledger - Common server manages the entire process

Distributed ledger - No single point of storage

Blockchain facilitates distributed digital ledgers with its decentralized nature. A distributed ledger is a database that is contemporized and accessed across different areas. Any change made by one user will be automatically reflected to all other connected nodes. That modification will be approved only if 51% of nodes accepting it. If not, that particular action will be prohibited. This process reduces cyber attacks and fraudulent activities which might occur in the network. It has greater potential to restructure the way institutions, governments and corporations work. When we think about scalability, blockchain works very well and fast with the least number of users on the network. The greater number of nodes reduces the speed of the transaction \[[14](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r14)\].

 

### 3.3. Cryptography

Cryptography facilitates secure communication. The meaning for the term “crypt” is “hidden” and “graph” means “writing” i.e. hidden writing. Hence the communicated message will be understood only by an intended receiver. It uses two special keys called private and public keys along with two most specific features called encryption and decryption. Encryption is the process of converting plain text into ciphertext and decryption is the process of converting the ciphertext into plain text (readable form). This encryption and decryption will be done with the private and public keys of the node. Data Encryption Standard (DES), Triple DES, two fish encryption, Advanced encryption standard (AES), the RSA algorithm are some of the most commonly used encryption algorithms.

The public key cryptography and consensus mechanism of blockchain, applied by the crypto-currency transaction are unbreakable for any fraudulent transactions \[[15](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r15)\]. In cryptography and cybersecurity, a man-in-the-middle attack is considered a widely performed attack where the hackers can easily monitor or alter the contents of the communication. This can be avoided by including features like private and public keys for authentication, using sensors linked with communicating devices with the help of TensorFlow, Raspberry pi, to implement IoT and blockchain for enriching the level of security in a transaction and uses MQTT (Message Queuing Telemetry Transport for transferring message between devices \[[16](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r16)\].

 

### 3.4. Hashing

Blockchain is usually comprised of a list of blocks connected with each other. It is not possible to alter the order of the blocks as they are interlinked to each other. Each block has a unique hash value associated with it. The hash value of one block will be linked with the next block. Therefore, every block will have its own unique hash value along with the previous block’s hash value. Fig. ([**2**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f2)) depicts the structure of blocks with their hash values where there is no possibility to rearrange the order of blocks \[[17](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r17)\]. A series of hashing algorithms were proposed and named Secure Hash Algorithm (SHA). SHA-1 was announced in 1993 which produces 160-bit hash but proved to have weak collision avoidance.

SHA-2 was announced in 2001. SHA 2 includes SHA-224, SHA-256, SHA-384,SHA-512. They are named by considering the length of hash bit or message digest generated by them.

SHA-3 came into existence to tighten the security level. But it made the process slow in software. Though the concerned security was given, it was comparatively slower than SHA-2.

Hash: 3346 Hash: 5456 Hash: 8856

Previous Hash: 0000 Previous Hash: 3346 Previous Hash: 5456

![](Images/9781681088624-C7_F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
Blocks with their hash values. ![](Images/9781681088624-C7_F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Generated hash value for a given string “Integration of blockchain and IOT”. 

IoT with blockchain improves network connectivity using peer-to-peer communication directly without relying on any third party. Blockchain technology with its security features avoids cyberattacks \[[18](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r18)\].

SHA-256 is the most commonly used hashing technique. This algorithm returns a hexadecimal representation. Therefore, it returns 64-bit hash values for every given input irrespective of the size of an input. In Figs. ([**3**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f3)) and (**4**), we can see that the inputs are not in the same length, but the hash values are 64 bits.

![](Images/9781681088624-C7_F4.jpg)**Fig. (4))**  
Generated hash value for a given string “BC and IOT”. 

Also, it must be noted that the string in Fig. ([**4**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f4)) is a substring of Fig. ([**3**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f3)). But we could not find any resemblance in their hash value which seems to be completely unique. Every transaction in blockchain technology will be stored in all the nodes connected to it, which can be termed as a full node. In full mode, huge storage space will be consumed which may not satisfy few users. Therefore, Lightweight nodes have been created which send their data to the full nodes instead of maintaining all the duplicate records in it. This will be suggested for devices with less computational power \[[19](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r19)\].

 

### 3.5. Smart Contract

A smart contract is a digital contract with the security coding of a blockchain. They are almost similar to the traditional contract. These smart contracts will be executed exactly by the rules coded by the coder when it is created. It directly controls the transfer of digital assets between the nodes at certain conditions. In the case of IoT, we can maintain a smart contract between devices and gadgets which we are using in our day-to-day life. While considering the operational mechanism of smart contracts, we can observe that its life cycle into five stages \[[20](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r20)\].

Negotiation – Analyses the needs for implementing smart contract

Development – Plans for the functionalities to get implemented

Deployment – Creates the contract between the parties involved in the transaction

Maintenance –This phase manages and maintains the data associated with it.

Learning and self-destruction – Organizes the entire process in a systematic manner. Self-destruction can also be implemented once the entire process is done.

Smart contract promotes automation. Many companies and organizations are putting efforts to include smart contracts into their regular practice by ignoring the traditional contracts \[[21](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r21)\]. No mishandling can be done when we are relying on a smart contract. This can be implemented using solidity language in the blockchain. Solidity is specifically created for maintaining smart contracts.

## 4\. IoT Implementation

Blockchain technology can be integrated with the Internet of Things (IoT) to enhance its applications in the real world. In recent times, this technology has responded constantly to the issues related to privacy and security, trust, reliability, single point of failure, and scalability by empowering peer-to-peer contract behavior, without the intervention of third parties. To overcome the vulnerability in security issues, we use cryptographic concepts like private and public keys with Public Key Infrastructure (PKI) for the secure distribution of keys to generate and verify digital signatures \[[22](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r22)\]. It can be implemented in few fields where green engineering can also be achieved. Certain applications are as follows:

### 4.1. Data Storage In A Distributed Manner

Initially, data will be shared through a cloud, and the permission and access controls will be managed by a centralized server where there is a chance for a single point of failure. Hence, we can shift towards a decentralized storage network called the blockchain. It can collaborate with IoT devices like sensors which can identify and alert the users at the time of immoral activities. This alert system can be used in many real-time applications in our day-to-day life.

### 4.2. Decentralized Energy Production And Consumption

Usually, energy resources are produced in different locations and they can be consumed by the people irrespective of locations until the resources exist. This energy production requires authentication for the producers to incentivize them for their efforts. There is a chance to forge people without recognizing their work or people may consume the energy resource and deny/pretend as if they have not consumed it. In such cases, we can implement blockchain technology to have a transparent transaction.

 

### 4.3. Selective Offloading To Minimize The Data Path

The concept of decentralization minimizes the data path. It is essential to redesign the network architecture to randomly offload computing and storage devices connected with IoT in order to minimize the expense and resource utilization. Every transaction will be recorded in the distributed ledger which lets all the connected nodes to be aware of the entire structure. Each datum will be stored in all the nodes in an encrypted format which can be decrypted only by the authorized user.

 

### 4.4. Iot In Home Appliances

Nowadays, home appliances are getting connected to the internet to access from anywhere and anytime. It is necessary for things to have secure communication with their owner. We can implement blockchain technology in this area to provide authenticated service with no interference. In smart homes, every device connected to the internet will periodically store its data in cloud storage for better efficiency to provide details upon query by the authority. For example, the security camera can be accessed or viewed from anywhere by the owner of the house without any fear of intruders \[[23](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r23)\].

 

### 4.5. E-Governance

Government portals are fully centralized. It can include this technology to improvise its sectors in a more efficient way. It includes other biometrics, IT details, education system, and so on. This decentralized method vanishes a single point of failure and guarantees secure storage of data. This process also promotes green engineering by accomplishing many essential aspects of our environment. Nowadays, technologies are playing a predominant role in achieving the objectives of green engineering by reducing the human intervention where we may come across fraudulent activities. When we discuss e-governance with blockchain technology, e-tendering will be an essential step in its progress in the business field. E-tendering promotes the online bidding process by providing more trust and efficiency with its distributed nature \[[24](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r24)\]. Blockchain can be integrated with IoT in many fields of governance.

 

### 4.6. Education System

Education and employment longed to achieve a flawless confluence between students, educational institutions, and companies which essentially improve the efficiency and transparency of education and employment. Students can be the administrator for their own data in this technology.

![](Images/9781681088624-C7_F5.jpg)**Fig. (5))**  
Perspective views on education. 

So that, he can monitor the changes done to his record by an authorized user without permitting any intruders \[[25](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r25)\]. Education institutions are maintaining students’ credit systems to assess their regular activities on the campus with which they can grade the student’s overall performance. It is necessary to carry that information from one institution to another (wherever he/she goes) to showcase the credits gained by them. This can be done with the help of blockchain technology where data cannot tamper, accessible by authorized users, secure storage, and so on. Blockchain can also be used for accreditation of educational institutions where the complex task of verifying the quality and qualification of the staff employed. In Fig. ([**5**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f5)), it is depicted that the education system can be viewed from three perspectives. It can be accomplished by learners, academicians, and business people. Whatever might be the field, people willing to develop themselves will never stop learning. If an institute owns a channel for online classes, only authorized users can access it. This restriction can be provided by the institution while deciding the beneficiaries. Also, it is possible to implement this technology for copyright and the digital right protection for an individual’s work. As there are many chances for plagiarism to occur, we can able to avoid it by using these kinds of blockchain platforms for recognizing one’s efforts. In this era, pilot development is needed for technology deployment.

 

### 4.7. Healthcare

Blockchain holds enormous applications in the healthcare sector. As a decentralized and distributed technology, it serves in different fields of this sector. It mainly ensures secure data sharing, maintenance of the medical history of patients, fee transactions, drug supply chain, and so on. This data sharing can occur in two ways, either locally or globally. If a medical record of a patient is transferred from one clinic/hospital to another in the same location, then it is termed local sharing. If data gets shared from country to country, then it is known as global sharing. With this platform, there is no need for a patient to carry his/her entire medical records while travelling to some other places.

Table 3 **Blockchain-based applications for the healthcare sector.**  

| **Applications** || **Description** |
| --- | --- |
| Data sharing || It must provide security for all the stored data and it must be securely transmitted to the authenticated user as per the request. The data can also be shared globally. |
| --- | --- |
| Medical record maintenance || Every medical data must be securely available for constant support for patients irrespective of time. |
| Management of drug supply-chain || It includes the entire chain of a product starting from the base material. |
| Bill payments || All the transactions can be managed by blockchain technology to accomplish a flawless service. For example, medical billing. |
| Research or clinical trials || It must be accountable for pivotal clinical and research trials. |
| Accessibility || It gives assurance for patients in managing their health records with extremely safe access controls. |

  

Table ([**3**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7t3)) illustrates the applications of blockchain technology in the healthcare sector. All data will be securely available on this platform. Without the knowledge of a user, it is impossible for an intruder to collapse the data. Hence, patients are the owner of their medical records. The basic architecture of collaborating blockchain technology with its IoT presents four layers for its working.

![](Images/9781681088624-C7_F6.jpg)**Fig. (6))**  
Smart contract between healthcare people. 

The layers are 1) IoT at e-healthcare, 2) Platform for blockchain, 3) Connectivity and 4) IoT devices. Also, the nodes connected to this technology can either be a full node (by storing the entire copy of the blockchain) or a lightweight node (by storing only the current status and last recent transaction in the blockchain) \[[26](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r26)\]. Fig. ([**6**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f6)) illustrates the smart contract between the people of the healthcare sector where the data will be interconnected with respect to the patient’s knowledge. With the presence of this smart contract, we do not need to rely on any third party’s monitorization. Blockchain technology provides a personalized platform to store all the health records under a single roof by promoting record tracking and accessing \[[27](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r27)\].

 

### 4.8. Secure Payment Processing

Blockchain technology agrees to provide secure, fast, minimal charge for national and international payment processing services by utilizing the encrypted distributed ledgers which provide trusted real-time verification for transactions without any intermediaries as they are maintaining smart contracts with the beneficiaries. Usually, the bank plays the centralized role in a transaction where a certain percentage of the amount will be automatically detected as a service charge. This can be avoided in the case of decentralization. Countries like Japan, Europe, China, and Texas have already introduced this technology in international payment processing and trade finance. As they are maintaining smart contracts, miner plays the role of checking authentication by collecting a very small unit of digital currency. Trade finance will be conducted *via* Digital Trade Chain Consortium in the form of Distributed Ledger Technology.

Industries also started accepting digital currencies using blockchain technology to avoid unnecessary charges and to provoke greater security. The following are some of the companies which widely accept these crypto-currencies.

**AIRFOX**: The company is located in Boston, Massachusetts. It facilitates the world to make payments, receive mini loans, and transfer money all over the world.

**CIRCLE**: It is located in Boston, Massachusetts. The circle pay function is accessible in 29 countries and in Euros, British pounds, and U.S. dollars. Every transaction is encrypted on blockchain to ensure safety.

**ZCASH**: It is located in Denver, Colorado. It has created its own crypto-currency with the name of zcash to help people in paying for goods and services.

**RIPPLE**: This company is located in San Francisco, California. The company’s RippleNet platform serves quick transactions of payments and requires a lower capital amount for cross-border payment.

**VEEM**: It is located in San Francisco, California. It provides blockchain-backed payment platforms to serve small businesses in sending and receiving money in local currency. It ensures a transparent history of all incoming and outgoing payments in small businesses too.

**IVY**: It is located in Beverly Hills, California. Businesses use IVY tokens to facilitate digital asset transformation and provide access to 24 know-your- customer (KYC) access points to have a transparent transaction from the secure access points.

This technology supports the growth of existing business models using cryptographically secured transactions in a distributed P2P network. In the financial sector, we have efficient platforms like Hyperledger Fabric, Ethereum, Corda, FinTech, DLT (Distributed Ledger Technology), and so on \[[28](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r28)\]. Blockchain technology is entering into all sectors by extending its wings to bring betterment to our society.

 

### 4.9. Supply-Chain Management and Logistics

Blockchain does not fail to enter the field of commerce too. The term Supply-chain management refers to the management of goods and services and also the processes involved in converting raw materials into a final product. By keeping this chain transparent, we can gain a competitive advantage in the market. The supply chain usually collides with business functions among the network of its trading partners. This may be vulnerable to certain attacks or risks to occur in the transaction. Hence, risk assessment is essential and it can be done by following the upcoming steps:

 

*   Initially, supply chain partners or traders must be identified. Then the contracts will be built through them.
*   Prepare a process map that shows all the information related to the transaction and its flow in the network. This helps to identify the risk factor.
*   Classify and observe the vulnerabilities
*   Develop an action plan to overcome the risk by including each step to be included in our process like RFID (Radio Frequency Identification) which spots the risk and captures the data automatically according to the vehicle movement.
*   Finally, implement the developed plan into reality to monitor and control risk factors in the supply chain \[[29](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r29)\].

This supply chain can be clubbed up with blockchain technology to provide the best service to the users. Initially, let us see four main functionalities of blockchain as follows:

**Transparent transactions**: Blockchain has no intermediaries. Also, it does not have any central authority to act as a server. Hence, it is completely transparent.

**Preapproved transaction fees:** While making a cross-border payment, the commission for a transaction (transaction fee) will be deducted only at the end of the process. Also, this transaction fee will be initially shown to the user and deducted only after the transaction gets successfully completed.

**Auditability:** Every transaction is instantly visible to all authorized parties which means, it is tamper-proof and no one can delete or modify the data associated with it.

**Reliability**: As it maintains a distributed digital ledger, there is no single point of failure. Hence they are immutable and eliminate the risks of fraudulent behavior. It includes three major use cases.

**Traceability** – It is essential to track the product from its base to the final outcome. IT helps in ensuring its standard.

**Transparency** – By capturing the data points like certifications and claims, it enables trust, and also open access will be provided to authorized users. Everything will be registered on a blockchain platform where the verification has to be done. These kinds of verifications are carried out by miners.

![](Images/9781681088624-C7_F7.jpg)**Fig. (7))**  
Working procedure of coffee supply chain with blockchain. 

**Trade ability** – It is a key concept of tokenization where the token value will be assigned for each property and stakeholders in a specific range. Blockchain technology assures ownership and license for a product. These tokens are tradeable with which the owner of an asset can transfer the property’s ownership without the physical asset changing hands.

**For example**, IBM Coffee machine. Yes. IBM blockchain has designed a machine using its application to track its entire supply chain. Once we requested a supply chain, it shows the details of the process from the date and place of the coffee bean’s growth, grinding, and filling it into the machine, and all these details are clearly shown to the customer. Fig. ([**7**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f7)) shows the steps involved in the coffee supply chain using blockchain technology. It has to be noted that every block is broadcasted and validated by everyone. Logistics is the process of detailed organizing and performing tasks like transferring products or goods from one location to another. It can be accommodated with blockchain technology with respect to its end-to-end delivery to proceed in a more secure way. This supply-chain management and logistics can be done in many sectors like the food value chain, finance and payment, healthcare, power generation fields, real estate, and so on.

 

### 4.10. Smart City

A smart city can be defined as an urban area that uses information and communication technology (ICT) in a more efficient way to enhance its operational efficiency. The utilization of ICT can be combined with blockchain technology to promote green engineering in many ways. Smart city includes many applications like:

*   Smart vehicles
*   Digital Identity
*   Smart parking
*   Waste management
*   Smart parking
*   Smart lighting
*   Smart sanitation and sensor utilizations
*   Smart environment

 

#### 4.10.1. Smart Vehicles

It includes many features like evidence catcher, prevents accidental situation, vehicle tracker (with GPS), and so on. In the case of the evidence catcher, consider an example where two vehicles are met with an accident and a mistake was on both sides. No one volunteers to be a witness to an accident. In such a case, a camera affixed to a vehicle covers the incident and pays a loyalty solution. Even then, there is a chance of losing the camera’s data with an intruder’s effort. To avoid this, we can make use of blockchain technology where there is no single point of failure. Secondly, we can track every vehicle through GPS. Instead of waiting for public vehicles like buses, we can track it on our own to reach the vehicle on time. It offers uninterrupted tracking of passengers or vehicles. Also, all information about the driver and the vehicle can be clearly tracked in a secure platform for future references. On other hand, vehicle sharing can also be promoted during transportation \[[30](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r30)\]. In order to keep it highly secure, blockchain technology is taken into consideration. An example of a smart city is Estonia. It includes all the features of a smart city at its best.

 

#### 4.10.2. Digital Identity

Nowadays, technological involvement has become increasingly common everywhere. Digital identity refers to an identity of an authorized user \[[31](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r31)\]. In the blockchain, it can be provided by the two special keys named private and public keys. The private key is to identify a single user. It will never be shared with others. The public key will be provided to all the users of a community to share with each other while having transactions. The public key of the users will be known to each other. Authentication is necessary for all the fields like healthcare, banking, transportation, asset maintenance, and so on. This process of providing keys for identification will be implemented by blockchain technology to own a secure digital identity. This identification can be made common for all the purposes of blockchain usage depending on the requirements.

 

#### 4.10.3. Smart Parking And Lighting

This includes the combination of blockchain and the Internet of Things (IoT) in order to increase smartness in public activities. Vehicles can be attached with sensors and cameras, to automatically drive and park the vehicles. In such cases, it is not necessary to depend on the drivers. Smart roads can be implemented in order to timely inspect the conditions of the road and its facility to avoid road accidents. This smart parking requires certain entities like KDC (Key Distribution Center), parking lot owners, PIR (Private Information Retrieval) along with a consortium blockchain network. KDC plays a vital role in invoking a smart parking system with this technology where the key generation will be done for authentication of vehicle, drivers, owners of parking lots, *etc.*, Payment processing for the lots can also be done for this parking system \[[32](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r32)\]. Also, street lights can be affixed with sensors to automatically on and off lights when the target time reaches. These applications will reduce unnecessary manpower by promoting green engineering. The services will be mainly shared among three participants, namely, owning organization, technology, and human.

Fig. ([**8**](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7f8)) depicts that all the three will be interconnected with each other to share the data or information within them. Organizing party interconnects with the stakeholders dynamically \[[32](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r32)\]. It gives transparency and protects from liability, unskilled service providers, and fraudulent activities. The technology assures the availability and accessibility of the systems and also real-time awareness and smart computing technology will be taken into progress. Human power trusts the security and privacy policies and believes in the reliability of sharing services. This can be achieved by blockchain technology.

 

#### 4.10.4. Waste Management And Smart Sanitation

It is possible to generate blockchain-based waste management and sanitation system. Waste management can be classified into two phases. The first phase includes collection and transport. It is the phase where the garbage and waste materials are collected and shifted to their destination in a regular interval. The second phase includes disposal and recycles where the collected trash will be vanished or treated for further use \[[33](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r33)\]. Waste management supports smart sanitation by considering this technology along with the Internet of Things (IoT). Tracking can be familiarly done with the help of IoT in a distributed platform to follow sanitation practices. In this portal, we will be having users like customers, municipality employees, supervising inspectors, contractors, police, and admin. These members will be assigned to one group or zone. This can be done for all the zones to connect the progress. The inspection process must be carried out at a regular interval. If not, people can file a complaint with the proof available in distributed ledger technology (DLT) against the inspector or any other employee who is responsible for the fault. In the waste management sector, it is indispensable to monitor the full cycle from how they have been gathered to how they are being discarded. Regular inspection helps in maintaining our environment in a clean and neat manner. Every data must be tracked using blockchain technology, which can not be altered. This procedural activity reduces the involvement of manpower and promotes green engineering.

![](Images/9781681088624-C7_F8.jpg)**Fig. (8))**  
Sharing services of smart city.  

#### 4.10.5. Smart Environment

We can integrate this blockchain technology with IoT to provide great inventions in favor of our society which facilitates a smart environment. It accumulates identification and alarming of forest fire, excessive air pollution (can attempt to control CO[2] emission from factories and toxic gases from vehicles), early detection of earthquake and landslide and monitoring the snow level to get escaped from natural calamities. This is slightly an advanced feature to implement in real life with the inclusion of blockchain technology. Also, it helps in creating awareness to people regarding the controlling measures that can be followed to minimize the level of pollution in our environment. It paves way for Industry 4.0 by enhancing its benefits for industries like manufacturing or production. Without neglecting the transportation sector, blockchain and IoT provides the best, even in the case of TDG (Transportation of Dangerous Goods) \[[34](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r34)\]. Hence, it is clear that it promotes technical properties in real-time by sharpening its security level.

 

#### 4.10.6. Blockchain In Tourism And Travel Management

Blockchain in tourism is still in its infant stage. Even in this stage, 13 platforms have been created and developed their own coins or tokens. Few of them have been created based on ethereum and few with their own format. DApps are considered the latest application of blockchain technology \[[35](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r35)\]. Tourism companies are also taking efforts to implement DApps to have better interaction with their customers. For example, Travel Block is a coin designed by the tourism sector based on ethereum with low cost as a highlight for their business models. In travel management, the ticket booking process for all kinds of transport vehicles can be done in a secure way without any interruptions with the help of blockchain technology. Process tracking and scheduling process can also be done with their transparent nature. The travel plan of the customers can be securely maintained in the travel management system of the company. This prevents crashes during travel with the help of the automatic reporting system. Entire tour details of the customers they served have to be completely managed and maintained by Travel Advisory System (TAS) in a blockchain platform for further reference \[[36](#9781681088624-C7.html#c7r36)\].

 

## CONCLUSION

In this chapter, we have proposed the benefits of integrating blockchain technology with the Internet of Things (IoT) from the basics by explaining aspects, including blockchain platforms, consensus, crypto-currencies, digital ledger, hashing, along with their application areas. It also explains the security measures considered with these technologies to enhance its performance in all aspects. With this integration, we can clearly conclude that there will be no single point of failure and no possibilities for repudiation. Hence, accessing our data or records from any device irrespective of time and location will be supported without bothering about data security. These two integrated technologies can fix any field like supply chain, healthcare sector, education, smart city, travel management, and so on. These technologies promote abundant development in our environment in all aspects.

Many research works are conducting in various countries to merge this technology with others to increase the efficiency of the processes. IoT with blockchain technology has started penetrating almost all the fields in our day-to- day life. Many security measures are still considered to enrich the security of these blockchain-enabled IoT systems. As further work, blockchain with IoT can be implemented in all the fields without any interruption, as it is under progress in our country. Therefore, it must be included in all the sectors of our environment. Researchers can make it possible by implementing this technology in a user-friendly manner in daily life in order to preserve the record in a secure way. We can conclude that the development and deployment of blockchain technology with other technologies like Artificial Intelligence, Machine Learning, Internet of Things, and Image processing are essential to address the specific demands of our future.

# Secured IoT Model for Sensitive Data Transmission Using Blockchain Technique

  
  
**Vejendla Lakshman Narayana^([1](#9781681088624-C8.html#aff1), [\*](#9781681088624-C8.html#cor1))****,** **R.S.M. Lakshmi Patibandla^([2](#9781681088624-C8.html#aff2))****,** **Arepalli Peda Gopi^([1](#9781681088624-C8.html#aff1))**  
¹ Vignan’s Nirula Institute of Technology & Science for Women, Peda Palakaluru, Guntur-522009, Andhra Pradesh, India  
² Department of IT, Vignan’s Foundation for Science, Technology and Research, Andhra Pradesh, India  
  

## Abstract

Global progress in terms of technology involves dumb physical objects that communicate and solve intelligent species problems for us. The thrilling world of the 'network of things' or the Internet of Things (IoT) is certainly a safe place to feel and fulfill our needs. Communication technologies for machine-to-machine(M2M) enable autonomous networking between devices without human intervention. Such autonomous control is of vital importance for a range of Internet of Things (IoT) implementations, including smart manufacturing applications, healthcare systems, and home automation, to name a few. The Internet of Things has attracted almost everyone's attention because of its ability to track and influence the environment. One of the key features of any IoT system is its ability to connect and exchange data with other devices. In particular, IoT devices are using wireless networking to communicate with other devices. In this test, it tells us how IoT technology can communicate with our lives. The proposed method uses Secured IoT Model for Efficient Data Transmission using Blockchain Technique(SIoTM- EDTB) to provide security to the data during transmission. The proposed model is compared with the traditional method, and the results depict that the proposed model exhibits better performance.

**Keywords:** Attacks, Blockchain, Data loss, Data transmission, Security model.  
  

* * *

## 1\. INTRODUCTION

The IoT is a new computing paradigm aimed at turning ordinary daily objects into intelligent objects. IoT was described as one of today's transformative innovations that will change our vision, interpretation, and reaction to the changes in the environment around us. Advancements in omnipresent and widespread compu-

ting, integrated devices, communication, Sensor networks, and Internet-based protocols underlie the development and enablement of IoT in ordinary devices \[[1](#9781681088624-C8.html#r1)\]. As a result, these technologies are usually known as technology enabling IoTs \[[2](#9781681088624-C8.html#r2)\]. The authors examine, with a special focus on their characteristics, capabilities, strengths, and issues of different wireless communication technologies widely used in IoT devices.

The IoT paradigm is not a new technology but rather a combined 20 nations of approaches that benefit from research developments in semi-controllers, networking, and the processing of information \[[3](#9781681088624-C8.html#r3)\]. If you are able to take a data center, you can use IoT to produce context-centric data and transmit it to another device or cloud where it is processed and mined to extract secret knowledge \[[4](#9781681088624-C8.html#r4)\]. If you are able to accept the data center, you can use IoT to create new devices as well as new physical objects. This will then enable new technologies and applications to be created. In facilitating the wireless sharing between the IoT devices and the portal, and then the gateway to the remote repository via Internet, Machine-to-Machine (M2M) communication technologies play a vital role. In view of the limited battery capacity of IoT equipment, it is clear that the energy efficiency of wireless communication is very important: both duty cycle and energy-recovery solutions are extensively examined.

The Internet of Things is focused on conventional telephone networks and other knowledge providers. IoT is an extension of the Internet. The Internet terminal is a computer (PC, server) running all sorts of programs \[[5](#9781681088624-C8.html#r5)\]. The Internet is nothing more than the encoding and transmission of data between the machine and the network \[[6](#9781681088624-C8.html#r6)\]. There is no other terminal (hardware) on the Internet. The Internet is still the main concept for IoT. Unlike the Internet, there are not only PCs and servers, but even built-in computer systems and their help sensors can be viewed as terminals \[[7](#9781681088624-C8.html#r7)\]. It can link all sorts of independent objects and make them work together in order to achieve a functional interconnection network.

Blocks are the technology's main principles. In the scheme, there are small collections of transactions. Each new block contains a reference to the previous transaction, including the previous transaction's SHA-256 hash \[[8](#9781681088624-C8.html#r8)\]. This creates a 'chain' of connections . Blocks are difficult to build computationally and need several specialized processors and a considerable amount of time to produce. Due to the difficulty in creating a block and to the interference from one block, you need to manipulate the previous block and follow the chain to alter it altogether.

The proposed model introduces Secured IoT Model for Efficient Data Transmission using Blockchain Technique. In the proposed model, data transmission is secured as the blockchain technology is used for locking the completed transmission so that nodes' behavior is also monitored \[ [9](#9781681088624-C8.html#r9)^(\-)[12](#9781681088624-C8.html#r12) \]. The introduction section gives a brief introduction of IoT and Blockchain and the remaining chapter is organized as a literature survey and then the proposed model that explains the process of secure data transmission framework, and then results are discussed and finally the conclusion of the chapter is given.

## 2\. LITERATURE SURVEY

The new wireless communications system for green intelligent cities has been proposed by Bedogni *et al*. \[[6](#9781681088624-C8.html#r6)\]. As IoT devices are used to plan, manufacture information, and monitor the energy supply and demand of households, IoT plays a key part in their plan. Although it was not mainly aimed at analyzing the communication systems used in IoT, they were assessed because they played a major role in the final implementation. The evaluation conducted in that work is quite superficial and focuses not on contact but the transfer of wireless power \[[13](#9781681088624-C8.html#r13)^(\-)[15](#9781681088624-C8.html#r15)\]. Moreover, not all available technologies are taken into consideration for assessment since they primarily focus on the SDN (SDN) architecture for heterogeneous IoT applications.

The study examined systems like cellular networks, short-range multi-shop technologies, and low-power (LPWA) technologies in order to use them for intelligent city implementation. The key weakness of these communication technologies is the failure to implement large-scale in restricted fields \[[16](#9781681088624-C8.html#r16)\]. It was also suggested that these limitations can be resolved with various network optimization methods, including cell zooming, HetNets implementation, and context-aware content delivery \[ [17](#9781681088624-C8.html#r17)^(\-)[19](#9781681088624-C8.html#r19) \].

The survey is similar to our study, although it has quite a different subject since it is based primarily on cellular technology and examines in detail the state-of-art criteria for 5 G; our work offers, compared to this, a more general classification of the M2M scenario (*e.g*. in the light of shorter distance 110 and capillary technology). Data sensor management and information extraction are very important in the second group \[ [20](#9781681088624-C8.html#r20)^(\-)[22](#9781681088624-C8.html#r22) \].

The industry has been carefully studying and classifying industrial context-conscious technologies and applications ranging from localized products to manufacturing and healthcare \[ [23](#9781681088624-C8.html#r23)^(\-)[25](#9781681088624-C8.html#r25) \]. Crowdsensing techniques are explored on the convergence of IoT, context-aware computing, and mobile devices.

## 3\. PROPOSED MODEL

Blockchain – a decentralized dispensed headline – is a breakthrough technology capable of addressing IoT security challenges. A blockchain-based IoT network approach will solve numerous problems facing the current model and increase security.

Blockchain has the following features as an important instrument in the fight against IoT cyber threats.

 

### 3.1. Decentralizing

Data is saved at various nodes around the world on the blockchain ledger, preventing a single failure point. Every node must accept and validate it before adding any data to the network. Therefore, no change is permitted without the general approval of all network participants. This method is called peer communication and is aimed at ensuring blockchain transactions by malicious parties. There is no risk that an individual will perform the middle assault if the hackers record data and sent it from the server to the computer because there is no single server.

 

### 3.2. Media Connections

Blockchain is public and is open to everyone on the network. The common history of stored blocks and transactions can be seen by all network participants, but a private key is required for access. This provides full access to all activities while maintaining secure data. It cannot be altered if a piece of information is stored in a blockchain.

 

### 3.3. Safe Information

In order to protect data, blockchain uses advanced encryption algorithms privately. This is done mainly to ensure that there is no risk to financial activities. IoT devices can send and receive information in the way financial transactions allow secure data communication between connected objects using the blockchain model.

The security of IoT is a major issue of privacy. Free Internet access to data allows users to be collected and monitored by monitoring agencies without their consent. The recent growth of IoT can easily lead to undesirable circumstances by data collection activities. One viable solution is to enable users to control their own data and to ensure confidentiality. This defines loosely how much data these programs need to work properly or in all the situations is actually agreed by the customers. On the other hand, in our environment, IoT devices often abuse the concept by collecting information without user permission.

Therefore, greater protection of privacy is needed to maintain the privacy of IoT users ' personal information. A new solution to limit user contacts to share the minimum amount of information needed is the Delegation Method. Let consumers find others on the platform that best suits their preferences without corresponding to their preferences.

To reveal to everybody this knowledge. Another concept underway is the privacy coach, in which the essence of this concept lies in a system that can scan and retrieve tags of embedded devices. If the object's privacy policy does not correspond to the user's preferences, the user refuses to use the object. In other circumstances, scanned items explicitly transmit their privacy policies and request the users' consent. The privacy coach will act as a physical space, malicious, and unwanted scanning defensive mechanism.

Various attack forms are based on a Cisco model because it is a common model that is very similar to the ISO / OSI model. The below-mentioned attacks can be perpetrated and presented a real challenge on Smart Home devices.

 

### 3.4. Edge Nodes Assaults

This section provides an in-depth overview of different attacks, including nodes, against the first layer of the reference model.

 

### 3.5. Denial of Installations

Denial-of-service attack, shortened by do-s, is normally a type of attack that usually inundates traffic-driven servers, devices, or networks to overload victims 'resources and prevent legitimate users from using them. DoS maybe three different attacks in the IoT world against edge computing nodes.

Battery drainage: IoT device size limits lead to small, very limited capacity batteries. Battery drainage attacks have become a powerful tool, leading indirectly to serious implications such as node failure and function failure. For instance, the entire fire alarm system will be disabled if an intruder discovers a way to deplete the battery of an intelligent SMD detector. If no battery in such a system can be replaced or recharged, an attacker could cause a node to be destroyed in the network. It can be done by sending a large number of arbitrary traffic to a node and forcing it to perform controls, such as authentication and checksum of packets.

Deprivation of sleep: is a specific type of DoS attack vaguely similar to the attack on battery drain by the battery of the node. Battery-powered devices with limited energy capacity are vulnerable to this attack. The attacker sent a valid number of requests in a timely manner to avoid sleep or twelve node functions. The battery-driven attack, because the packets are actual, is a much harder one to detect. External attacks are the most common DoS type for stopping edge nodes and forcing an outage. It prevents machines from operating normally and, in some cases, shuts off operations completely. This can be done because of accidental fabrication errors, the battery attacks mentioned above, unauthorized physical access to the device, or the injection of code. Stuxnet, which has inserted a malicious code that avoided an abnormative behavior in Iran that could not shut down in a state of emergency, is a well-known example of a failed attack.

 

### 3.6. Strong Node Management

Access to the network physically is an opening for further attacks. Direct node manipulation attacks can be divided into two categories according to the type of operation carried out by the attack node. Each such attack may cause severe damage and often lead to considerable network performance reduction. Node replication is an attack-type where an attacker replicates a node identification number by adding a new node to an existing set of nodes. Newly added nodes are often malicious, which makes it easy for the attacker to corrupt or misdirect the replica packets. This attack allows the assailant to access cryptographic shared keys or, by executing node revocation protocols, to even relocate access to valid and authorized nodes.

Camouflage attacks are used to insert or hit a node that is accepted to hide on the edge. This malicious node is used in normal mode in which packets identified as active are received, processed, or sent back. Only traffic analysis and data collection are performed through passive mode. Node functions are defined. Node corruption is a common attack to access the underlying node network unauthorized. Malicious nodes inserted into the network will gain access to other nodes which could control the whole system for the assailant. The malware node may also be used to insert false data in the device or prevent true messages from being transmitted.

 

### 3.7. Attacks Routing

Attacks in the routing process ask how packets are routed to the communication layer. An attacker can spoof, redirect, error, or completely drop the packet out of the network by such an attack. The routing information can be simply changed to create routing loops or error messages. Routing attacks can be subsequently split into several different categories.

The Gray Hole Attack is called a variation of the attack, in which nodes selectively drop certain packets. Wormhole attacks are much more dangerous than Black and Gray Hole attacks since they can be performed in all communications even with authenticity and secrecy. The heart of this attack is that packets are received and then tunneled to another location in the network. Basically, wormhole nodes create away in a network that is shorter than the original. Therefore, the tunnel must be a quick connection to create the illusion that both wormhole nodes are very similar.

 

### 3.8. Solution IoT Blockchain

It should be noted that in particular when the global P2P framework develops across the entire IoT ecosystem, the above mechanisms (P2P network involvement, support of acceptable P2P protocols, peer-functionality endorsing, support for consensus algorithms, PoW, and other associated mechanisms) give rise, are somewhat complex. Because of the conventional limitations of IoT nodes as set out in section 2, the use of a fully-fledged blockchain network within an IoT generic framework may not always be feasible; certain key or institutional applications such as smart grids, ITSs, e-health, insurance, or smart contract environments may, however, have sufficient capacity to support the required functions of P2P. Another alternative is to establish P2P networks with a limited local range instead of a global one; this requires less consolidated bandwidth and far-reaching assurance of a stable distribution over large networks for the messages supported – finding, transaction querying, invocation, synchronization, and consensus.

### The Stage of The Gateway:

Individual users generate data not always secured for integrity, but once the data enters the gateway, it is integrated with data from other users into the blockchain.

### The Photo Picture:

This is a case in point. One reason is that there is no computing capability to generate hashes of (possibly large) data blocks for individual end nodes.

### Standard Website:

In this case, individual website users (*e.g*. sensorsorrobotsonthe floor) generate data that isn't automatically secured for system integrity. However, the data are integrated into the blockchain along with information from other users of the website once the data entered into the local focus node (*e.g*. layer 2 switch, Wi-Fi connection point, router, firewall, *etc*.). One reason for this approach is because individual end nodes cannot compute (possibly large) blocks of data, but the NE based on the site will be capable of computing.

 

### 3.9. Strong IoT Protection with Blockchain Technology

For the stable application of IoT, the following points should be considered.

 

#### 3.9.1. Secure Communication

In order to process the transaction, the IoT devices must communicate and save it in a repository. Ledgers can also be used to store encryption keys to ensure secure transfers. An encrypted message is sent to the IoT system via a public key of the destination device that is then saved on a blockchain network. The sender then asks his node to obtain the public key of the recipient from the headline. And the transmitter encrypts the message with the public key of the recipient, so only the recipient can decrypt the message with his private key.

 

#### 3.9.2. Consumer Authentication

Before sending it to other users, the sender will sign the message digitally. In order to validate the digital signature of the received letter, a receiver would then receipt the public key from the ledger. The following are the work on digital signatures:

*   Sender calculates the hash of a private key-encrypted message.
*   The message will be sent along with a digital signature.
*   The receiver will then decrypt the digital signature by means of a public sender key stored in the directory to retrieve the sender hash value.
*   The message is true only if the hash and the hash of the message that is measured are identical.
*   Recovered confidence is enhanced if each sender's digital signature is stored in the directory.

 

#### 3.9.3. Finding a Broad Legal IoT

Once a new IoT system is introduced, a list of confident nodes on the network is requested by root servers. This computer then registers in a node and the information exchange starts. In order to secure root server name resolution, DNSSec must be introduced, preventing attacks by spoofing. Any contact made must be effectively authenticated and encrypted. This can be done based on the following:

 

*   Credentials are already mounted during configuration on the computer.
*   The IoT device owner may issue credentials.

 

#### 3.9.4. IoT Configuration

Blockchain technology greatly helps to build a safe and reliable IoT device setup. Approaches that seem important in this regard are:

*   On a ledger, you can host IoT features, such as setup data and the latest version of the validated firmware. During bootstrap, your blockchain node must be set up from the ledger. The setting needs to be encrypted in the booklet so that information contained in the public leader does not discover the topology or its properties of the IoT network.
*   The hash value for each device can be stored in the ledger of the most recent configuration file. The IoT device has the latest and most trustworthy configuration file to be downloaded at every fixed time interval using a cloud service. The app can then retrieve and match the hash value stored in the database using a blockchain node API. Administrators are thus able to regularly uninstall bad settings and restart all IoT systems on the network with the latest and most trusted settings.
*   Securing the IoT network with a blockchain network is a decentralized system with no single authority able to approve any transaction. A copy of the ever-growing data chain will come from every computer. This means all network members must verify it whenever someone wants to access the system and transact. The transaction is placed in a block and forwarded to all the nodes on the network after confirmation. All this ensures that unauthorized sources do not violate security and make the system safer.

## 4\. RESULTS

The proposed model is implemented using an NS2 simulator for creating IoT nodes that are used for secured data transmission. The proposed model introduced a Secured IoT Model for efficient data transmission using Blockchain Technique. The proposed model is compared with the traditional models and the results show that the proposed model exhibits better security in the transmission of data using the blockchain technique. The proposed model is compared with the traditional models in terms of data transmission rate, security levels, attack identification levels, accuracy levels, and throughput. The parameters used for creating an IoT simulation are depicted in Table **[1](#9781681088624-C8.html#t1)**.

| --- | --- |
| Area Size || 500 X 500 |
| MAC || 802.11 |
| Simulation Time || 200 sec |
| Traffic Source || CBR |
| Packet Size || 512 |
| Transmit Power || 1.500w |
| Receiving Power || 0.675w |
| Transmission Range || 100m |
| No.of Sinks || 5 |

  

The Data Transmission rate of the proposed and the Traditional Trusted Node-based Transmission Method (TTNTM) are depicted in Fig. (**[1](#9781681088624-C8.html#f1)**). The data transmission rate of the proposed model is high when compared to the existing method. As the proposed model considers only authorized nodes, the data transmission rate is high. All the nodes will transfer the data to its neighbor nodes without causing any data loss. The proposed model uses blockchain to record all the data transmission instances and then analyze the generated blocks to identify the nodes that are malicious.

## CONCLUSION

Internet of Things is the key path people can take. Some major companies are beginning to deliver various kinds of services to our everyday lives, such as Smart Home, Health Monitor, and so on, to improve the quality of human life by using the Internet of Things. It's a big step forward for computer science technology as a whole. However, it has two sides. There are several issues on which we can attract our attention to. As we all know, IoT is the next version of the Internet, but it's all focused on the Internet. The protection concern should still be an i mportant point that needs to be looked into. Telecommunications technology has also limited the growth of telecommunications. In addition, we should pay attention to problems, such as latency, transmission costs, and so on. An example of an IoT application has been listed in the article. It explained how a simple IoT might already have an effect on people's lives. However, attention should be given to future jobs. The cognitive IoT method is more complex, but it is more successful to see how IoT can accomplish the ultimate aim of the Internet of Things. The creation of a real cognitive IoT framework should be considered as a future task. In the future, a central head node can be assigned to analyze the remaining nodes so that the complexity and congestion in the network can be reduced.

# Transforming OTT Digital Platform Business Using Blockchain Technology

  
  
**J.S. Shyam Mohan^([1](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9aff1), [\*](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9cor1))****,** **Nagendra Panini Challa^([2](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9aff2))****,** **Pasumarthy Swathi^([1](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9aff1))****,** **Nuggu Kowshiki^([1](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9aff1))**  
¹ Department of Computer Science and Engineering, Sri Chandrasekharendra Saraswathi Viswa Mahavidyalaya (SCSVMV), Tamil Nadu 631561, India  
² Department of Information Technology, Sri Vishnu Engineering College for Women, Bhima-varam, Andhra Pradesh 534202, India  
  

## Abstract

Technology is becoming an indispensable part of our lives. It plays an important role in health, banking, and education. Organizations have started adopting these technological developments to satisfy customer needs. The rise of the Over-the-Top (OTT) digital platforms has created an economic platform in which the digital content, services, and offerings are facilitated by intermediaries and has changed the way of interaction. The blockchain serves as a means for creating a digital platform by creating trust among the intermediaries. Organizations should carry out innovative technology practices to be successful in a competitive environment. This chapter examines the gap between blockchain platforms and the role of the digital transformation process. It thereby provides researchers a holistic approach for studying the impact of blockchain on OTT platforms.

**Keywords:** Blockchain technology, Digital technology, Over-the-Top.

## 1\. INTRODUCTION

Over-the-Top (OTT) is a term used in the media industry when TV is used to reach individuals’ homes through physical links and boxes, and advanced content was conveyed through the web 'over the top' of the links and boxes. OTT has two products, the content and the viewers or the subscribers. Data collected from various locations is processed using Big Data analytics for enhancing and improving the customer viewing experience. Compared with steady competition from traditional television, the future of OTT platforms across the world looks bright.Traditional TV has started innovating by adapting to newer technologies to reach out to a wider range of audiences. Over the last decade, traditional TV has

started including OTT platforms as a part of their service offered to the consumers. AIB Research states that the video market across the world will keep on increasing at a fast pace with over-the – Top (OTT). By 2022, the OTT market will produce massive revenue of $51.4 billion with a surge CGAR (Compound Annual Growth Rate) of 10%. Subscribers will enjoy most of the benefits of OTT provided if they subscribe to the OTT platforms. In countries like North America and Europe, TV channel broadcasters are providing OTT services to their customers to withhold them consistently. Virtual Multichannel Video Programming (vMVPD) is provided at relatively cheaper prices to all the subscribers \[[1](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r1)\]. Globally, the number of subscribers opting for OTT is increasing when there has been a decline in traditional TV. The Average Revenue per User (ARPU) for pay channels is expected to go down four times, probably by 2022. Thus, there is no doubt that OTT players are marching towards increasing their revenue by adding more number of subscribers. Amazon, Apple, DAZN, Facebook, Google, and Netflix have started investing in setting up their own production houses. A blockchain describes an ever-growing set of shared, maintained, digital records and verified by several participants. There is a myriad of blockchain species, but this one definition is suitable for most scenarios \[[5](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r5), [6](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r6)\]. Each set of digital records in a blockchain is grouped into dependent blocks in order. This means that attempting to modify a transaction in any block would usually require modifying all of the following blocks, a computation-intensive task that is economically feasible by design, taking into account a new block is added within minutes. This process makes the data more sure and as time goes by, more blocks are added. Since this unique record set is maintained continuously in several places or distributed, it is highly fault tolerant; there is no central party that attacks, shuts down, coerced, or (in many cases) sues \[[7](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r7)\]. This decentralization is absolutely essential to do this work; a blockchain 100% owned by a single entity provides barely incremental benefit. Blockchain technology owes its origin to the Bitcoin cryptocurrency. Ensuring consent around a permanent digital ledger without the participation of a trusted central authority was needed (such as a bank, e-payment provider, or government) in order to create “digital currency” \[[8](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r8), [9](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r9)\]. Without an authoritative party to keep the register, you had to design something really innovative so that someone couldn't copy and paste the way they spend their digital money twice. This feature of blockchain technology, or the ability to have a unique and truly owned digital resource, is a vitally important characteristic for brands and marketers. The “digital scarcity” of the blockchain technology not only supports the value of Bitcoin, but it also provides brands with a real bridge between the finite physical world and a numerically infinite world. Many companies see blockchain as an open platform \[[10](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r10)\]. Open blockchains, like those that support Bitcoin cryptocurrency and Ethereum network, rely on a new model to make up for the lack of a trusted broker. On the other hand, with their exploration of closed or “authorized” blockchains, financial institutions are fall to the top to adopt a technology that an anonymous individual or group invented to help render these institutions useless after the 2008 crash. Even in this digitally sterile implementation, banks can enjoy advantages such as quick settlement times and more quickly agreed trade finance terms by inheriting at least some of the characteristics of an open blockchain, such as immutability and decentralization.

## 4\. DEFINING BLOCKCHAIN

A Blockchain is the data store that is distributed across a network consisting of many participants that rely on consensus based on the validity of the transactions without any central authority. In the blockchain, the transactions cannot be altered or modify as blockchain is a decentralized, distributed, and public ledger system. A blockchain is managed by anonymous users. Every user can check the data and verify or discard using consensus algorithms that cannot be altered \[[16](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r16)\]. For ensuring secure, transparent, and immutable transactions, a cryptocurrency uses blockchain technology, and securing data in IoT is successfully handled by blockchain technology \[[17](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r17), [18](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r18)\]. The working principle of blockchain is shown in Fig. ([**1**](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9f1)). All the transactions happen *via*. smart contracts in blockchain (section 3.1). Working of blockchain and its attributes, applications can be found in \[[19](#9781681088624-C9.html#c9r19)\]. Steps involved in blockchain are given below:

![](Images/9781681088624-C9_F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Working of Blockchain. 

1.  A buyer buys a product online and makes a transaction.
2.  Transactions in a blockchain are represented as blocks.
3.  All the transactions in the blocks are broadcasted in the peer-peer network.
4.  Blockchain network checks the buyer’s identity and validates once verified.
5.  All the verified transactions are added as a record and are added to the blockchain.
6.  Finally, the transaction is completed as the payment moves to the seller.
