## 1\. INTRODUCTION

Blockchain and Big Data are two technologies that continue to expand at a phenomenal rate. Both have been developed separately and have largely been applied in various industries independently from each other. Blockchain applications have been centric towards cryptocurrency and bitcoins with their distributed ledger technology (DLT) attracting both tech enthusiasts and potential investors in this new area of business. It has further expanded into other fields such as banking and supply chain management, finding use in all spheres of life. Blockchain technology truly can disrupt the current economic system of the world by its decentralized structure and removal of the third party. Big Data has become a buzzword in recent years with data multiplying like never before. Its applications cannot be limited to just one field as data is utilized and generated everywhere. From sensors to social media websites, big data is everywhere around us. Challenges arise when it comes to utilization and analysis as it holds the key to vital information. This information invariably proves to be valuable to businesses and governments alike, studying the performance of their products and services.

The major contribution of the following chapter will be highlighting the:

*   linking up of the two powerful technologies Blockchain and big data.
*   focusing on how Blockchain technology can change the way we deal with big data and make it a much more efficient process.
*   highlighting the variety of benefits such as ensuring data security and performing data analysis, - Blockchain’s advent and its integration with big data can serve a variety of industries ranging from finance to medicine.

The paper is organized as follows. The introduction includes Mechanism, Contemporaneous advancements in blockchains, and the challenges it must face, it also includes the basics of big data, its types, and characterization which are focused on in the second part of the same section. Section 2 comprises how big data and blockchain can together make a difference. Section 3 highlights the applications when the two technologies are used together. The final two sections are completely dedicated to further advancements and the conclusion.

## 2\. Introduction to Blockchain

Blockchain technology \[[1](#9781681088624-C11.html#r1)\] has revolutionized the world in more than one way. It is safe to say that it has covered multiple and a variety of spheres ranging from the economy \[[2](#9781681088624-C11.html#r2)\] to healthcare. The history of how blockchain came to be can be credited to Satoshi Nakamoto, who outlined the basic workings of the technology and since then it has continued being developed for a variety of applications. Ethereum took things a step further and developed the concept of smart contracts. Blockchain is often referred to as Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT). A simple way to look at it would be to take the example of a Google Doc or a Wikipedia Page. Here, our document is distributed amongst many and every person can make a change in real-time and any updates are visible and transparent to all. Since blockchain is based upon DLT and everyone has access to the ledger, the information is transparent thus increasing accountability \[[3](#9781681088624-C11.html#r3)\].

A major advantage that blockchain \[[4](#9781681088624-C11.html#r4)\] has over existing systems is that there are no transaction costs involved. Infrastructural costs to maintain the system exist but any user making any trade will not face any additional costs. This happens because blockchain cuts off any third parties owing to its decentralized system. This will have serious consequences in the future economic system considering that many businesses currently make a profit by being the third party connecting the consumer to the product. For example, a yearly subscription to any magazine will stop being popular as the user can just pay a very small amount per article considering no additional transaction costs will be levied.

Blockchain is quite literally what its name implies - a chain of ‘blocks.’ Here ‘blocks’ are digital pieces of information stored in a public database ‘the chain.’ Despite its growing significance in today’s world, blockchain is not a new technology. It is in fact an amalgamation of three proven and existing technologies \[[5](#9781681088624-C11.html#r5)\], explained in Fig. (**[1](#9781681088624-C11.html#f1)**).

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
3 technologies. 

The Peer to Peer Network is the defining feature of blockchain technology. The network is nothing, but a system of nodes connected and interconnected with each other. Each node is a computer network that can either take any input or perform functions and send its output. There is no hierarchy among different nodes and there is a uniform topology with the work being divided equally.

Public Key Cryptography \[[6](#9781681088624-C11.html#r6)\] uses the concept of two keys, one public and one private. The public keys are as the name suggests public and distributed amongst the members while the private key is possessed by a few. A public key can encrypt the message in a manner that only the person holding the private key can read it. The person possessing the private key can create a digital signature so that anyone with the relevant public key can validate the information.

The program refers to the protocol which is essentially a set of rules governing the p2p nature of the ledger. It covers aspects such as validation trade and listing down the mechanism for the blockchain to work. The protocol is run by a digital asset which creates an element of self-interest to keep the blockchain going. These digital assets are essentially rewarding in return for computing power used to mine. These can either be coins or tokens depending upon the scenario.

### 2.1. Mechanism

Just as three technologies form blockchain technology, three pillars are holding up the blockchain mechanism. These three pillars are immutability, decentralization, and transparency. Immutability refers to the non-tamper able nature of blockchain owing to its hash function. Decentralization speeds up the transaction process and provides better security and accountability. Transparency is an obvious consequence of the decentralized system. While it protects the private information of the users, the transactions are still made public making it a very safe system to work with. The basic mechanism behind the working of a blockchain can be broken down into three steps \[[7](#9781681088624-C11.html#r7)\]:

 

1.  Once someone requests a trade/transaction, the same is recorded. This block stores information such as the timestamp, trade details, and each party’s digital signature.
2.  The transaction is verified. This job is done by a network of computers (called nodes)
3.  This is stored in the form of a block among many other similar transactions. Each block has a unique code called ‘hash’. Despite the similarity of any two trades, every block has a unique hash. Each block is also given the hash of the previous block, thus forming a blockchain.

All the steps are shown in the Fig. (**[2](#9781681088624-C11.html#f2)**), making it clear with every step:

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
Blockchain formation. 

Since blockchain is completely decentralized, trust becomes a problem. Blockchain \[[8](#9781681088624-C11.html#r8), [9](#9781681088624-C11.html#r9)\] is open to everyone and each node remains anonymous thereby making it difficult to validate the information passed. Therefore, consensus models are developed to ensure trust in the newly decentralized system of nodes. There are two types of consensus models \[[10](#9781681088624-C11.html#r10)\]:

#### 2.2.1. Proof of Work

To add any block to the chain, the nodes must perform mining to validate the information and record the transaction. This involved solving a difficult computation puzzle that consumes a lot of power. In return, they receive rewards such as tokens or coins.

#### 2.1.2. Proof of Stake

This was created as an alternative to the Proof of Work model. It provides more security and less risk. According to this concept, users can buy tokens that allow them to mine or validate transactions. More coins or tokens lead to more mining power.

### 2.2. Types

The Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT) that blockchain works with is a boon to the modern world. It increases security \[[11](#9781681088624-C11.html#r11)\], improves accountability, and leads to greater transparency. It even cuts down the need for a third party hence reducing transaction costs. There are certain limits to it. DLT needs to be customized and worked upon according to the needs of the users leading to a need for categorizing different types of blockchains. There are four types of blockchains depending upon their use of Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT) \[[12](#9781681088624-C11.html#r12)\]:

#### 2.2.1. Public Blockchain

These blockchains are non-restrictive and permission-less. Anyone can be a part of the blockchain as a user or miner and transactions are completely transparent. Starting with public blockchain which is completely an open foundation. Public blockchain permits every user to participate in the system. These are created so that they are completely decentralized in nature, no user/miner has the right to control the recorded data in the chain formed. It is resilient to censorship as everyone is permitted to join the system irrespective of the location they are in, the network they want to fetch, *etc.* hence it makes it extremely difficult for the ruling classes to lock them down.

#### 2.2.2. Private Blockchain

These blockchains are restrictive in nature and are also referred to as permissioned blockchains. Consent is required to join the network and the transactions are visible to only the members of the blockchain. They are centralized in nature and authorization and accessibility controls are handled by an organization. An authenticated consent is needed for joining the network, only the participants that are associated with the network can access the data. These are comparatively more centralized. This type is highly used in cases where the data is a sensitive issue for the organization. There may be the involvement of a token in the system which will help in easy management of the entities fetching the data.

#### 2.2.3. Consortium Blockchain

These have semi-decentralized systems wherein instead of a single entity, a group of organizations governs the blockchain. It has a similar approach to that of a private blockchain and can be considered as a sub-type of the same.

#### 2.2.4. Hybrid Blockchain

A hybrid blockchain uses features of both private and public blockchains. Users are given the option of deciding which data goes up on a public platform and which data remains private. The transactions can either be verified within the network or on a public network, which increases the number of nodes for hashing thereby increasing the security of the system. The features of hybrid systems provide tractability at a significant amount and help in enabling easy connections with the protocols present in blockchain systems. Effectively used in various business aspects where the focus is on security and flexibility.

### 2.3. Contemporary Advancements in Blockchain

With Blockchain technology continuously blooming and growing, it continues to grow and expand in terms of applications and implementations. Blockchain has become a buzzword in the world of tech and all enthusiasts are in some way or the other part of it. After seeing more than 40 percent growth since 2018, the future of blockchain looks even more promising. The statistics are highly encouraging with job offers on LinkedIn tripling from 2017 to 2018. The cryptocurrency \[[1](#9781681088624-C11.html#r1)\] market was sized to be around 600 billion dollars and global spending on blockchain solutions estimated to be around 2.1 billion dollars in 2018. There has been a sharp increase in global interest related to blockchain since 2016 showing immense potential for further growth through raising awareness \[[8](#9781681088624-C11.html#r8), [9](#9781681088624-C11.html#r9), [13](#9781681088624-C11.html#r13), [14](#9781681088624-C11.html#r14)\].

While most players in the tech world already have their hands on blockchain technology, it is not without risks. Since it is a new and largely untested technology, introducing it to the already functional system in place might lead to breaches and security issues. Cloud providers with large and established customer bases seem to be at the forefront of including blockchain technology \[[13](#9781681088624-C11.html#r13)^(\-)[16](#9781681088624-C11.html#r16)\] into their business model. Microsoft Azure is one of the frontrunners in incorporating the technology by focusing on services such as Ethereum and Hyperledger Fabric methods. It has also built up the Azure Blockchain Service and Workbench. Amazon Web Services has also released Managed Blockchain to its public. IBM Cloud and Oracle blockchain platform are also basing their solutions on Hyperledger Fabrics, shown in Fig. (**[3](#9781681088624-C11.html#f3)**).

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Advancements made in sectors *via* blockchain. 

There are four major areas where contemporary advancements in blockchain technology are taking place \[[17](#9781681088624-C11.html#r17)\]. Platform advancements include the ability to sustain operations despite component failures, a support system for storing encrypted data, and smooth switching between the two consensus mechanisms. Interface developments comprise many changes such as developing a mechanism to authenticate using established identity providers and privacy and permission features for transactions. There is a need to develop an infrastructure that supports cross-network interoperability and a network capable of handling many nodes. As expected, most modern contemporary advances in blockchain technology are still targeted towards improving security and carrying out smooth analytics.

### 2.4. Challenges

Despite its innumerable benefits listed in section 2.3, blockchain technology is not without its challenges. While having features such as a decentralized system and data security \[[18](#9781681088624-C11.html#r18)\] which are the need of the hour, implementing these is a hard task. Since it is still a newly developed technology, some drawbacks need to be countered to develop a technology suitable for use in all spheres. Steps such as countering existing performance issues, improving privacy, and developing an easy-to-use platform are crucial before integrating blockchain into various applications. There are majorly four problems plaguing blockchain development \[[19](#9781681088624-C11.html#r19)\]:

#### 2.4.1. Increased Performance

While blockchain certainly has its advantages in carrying out transactions it still cannot compete with the existing systems. The key aspect turns out to be speed which is usually only 5-7 transactions per second in comparison to tens of thousands of transactions carried out by established systems. This rules out blockchain’s applicability \[[20](#9781681088624-C11.html#r20)\] in large-scale models. To tackle privacy issues consensus models are developed which again increase the transaction duration.

 

#### 2.4.2. Interoperability

Interoperability is probably the most important issue that needs to be tackled. Different blockchain platforms require translations to communicate \[[21](#9781681088624-C11.html#r21)\] with each other which proves to be a very cumbersome task. There is a need to develop a system of standardization to improve interconnectivity and facilitate communication.

 

#### 2.4.3. High Cost and Complexity

Another challenge faced by blockchain technology is its need for intensive computing. It consumes high electrical power leading to high costs. A recent study published by Elite Fixtures calculated the costs of mining one bitcoin in South Korea to be around 26000 dollars. Besides, there is the cost of developing and maintaining blockchain servers and networks.

 

#### 2.4.4. Lack of Supportive Regulation

The absence of supportive regulation certainly remains to be an issue. With cryptocurrency prices spiking recently and phenomena such as Initial Coin Offering (ICO) taking place, the speculative nature of the market is quite scary. In addition to that newly developed technologies relying on a blockchain, such as smart contracts don’t have any regulatory system in place. This inhibits both the developers and the users and discourages investments.

## 3\. INTRODUCTION OF BIG DATA

Big Data is the latest buzzword in technology taking tech enthusiasts and multinational companies by storm. It is no surprise that the world we live in is a data-driven and statistical analysis of this data is key to good governance, increased revenues, and overall a more productive society. With such a variety of applications and promising results, understanding big data has become more crucial.

First and foremost, it is imperative to understand what data is. Data is nothing but a set of characters, symbols, or quantities upon which a variety of operations are carried out and that can be transmitted in various formats. Big Data is essentially just data but a very large magnitude of it. It’s usually a humongous dataset that continues to grow exponentially over time. This makes the data very hard to work with as the normal operations we used to perform on normal data cease to work.

Big Data has become such an essential part of today’s world not only because of its applications but also because of its sheer growth. Around 90 percent of the world’s total data has been produced in the past two years alone showing its massive growth over the recent period. IBM estimates that 2.5 quintillion bytes of data are produced daily globally \[[22](#9781681088624-C11.html#r22)\]. These statistics effectively prove the need for big data analysis in contemporary times and the requirement for cost-effective yet efficient solutions for managing such data. Big Data includes not just the data but also its frameworks and tools required to work with it. Data mining, storage, scrapping, visualization, and all such operations come under the same heading.

The importance of big data in various industries cannot be ignored. The utilization of big data leads to the formation of effective business plans, thus giving companies a competitive advantage. It also helps give fruitful insights into customer activities leading to efficient marketing campaigns. This provides better customer service, improves products, and leads to higher conversion rates. This data can come from a variety of sources such as social networking sites, real-time data sensors, medical records, and more. This includes raw data which might be operated upon using tools such as data mining to make it more usable for analytics.

### 3.1. Types of Big Data

With big data being such an expansive field covering a multitude of operations, it comes as no surprise that there are a variety of datasets to be dealt with. Often, we can classify most of them into three categories \[[23](#9781681088624-C11.html#r23)\]:

 

#### 3.1.1. Structured

This refers to data that can be stored, retrieved, and processed in a fixed and specific format. This data is stored in an easy-to-read format which makes it easy to operate upon and derive information from the same. It is highly organized making basic operations such as storing and searching an easy task. With data growing exponentially converting data to this form proves to be a hard task. An example of such a data set would be an employee’s table in a company with predefined and organized rows and columns describing various attributes such as name, age, designation, and more \[[24](#9781681088624-C11.html#r24)\].

 

#### 3.1.2. Unstructured

Unstructured data refers to data lacking any specific form. It is raw data and because of its huge size and unstructured format, it is very hard to work with it. Deriving information, processing, and performing basic operations on this data is a difficult job despite the data being readily available to most companies. Email is a basic example of such a dataset.

 

#### 3.1.3. Semi-structured

This type of data contains components from both structured and unstructured data. Despite not being classified under a specific repository, it has some sort of tags to distinguish elements from each other. The data represented by an XML file is an example of the same.

### 3.2. Characterization of Big Data

Considering the popularity of Big Data in modern times, it was necessary to have certain defining characteristics. The characteristics help identify and categorize the data sets as required by the user. In 2001 Doug Laney, an analyst at Gartner identified the 3 V’s of big data, namely volume, variety, and velocity with the volume being the key characterizing component. Further studies and research lead to the listing of three more V’s namely variability, veracity, and value \[[25](#9781681088624-C11.html#r25)\] shown in Fig. (**[4](#9781681088624-C11.html#f4)**).

 

#### 3.2.1. Volume

The volume of data is what classifies any data as big data. An enormous volume of data signifies that the dataset is big data. This huge volume of data can come from any source such as business, machines, humans, and a myriad of other sources.

 

#### 3.2.2. Variety

Variety refers to the variety of sources and the types of data being processed. This could be structured, unstructured, or semi-structured data. Earlier data was generally available in clean spreadsheets and databases but now data is extracted in various forms such as PDFs, emails, photos, and more. This makes analysis, mining, cleaning, and other operations on such data a tedious task.

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F4.jpg)**Fig. (4))**  
6 V’s of big data.  

#### 3.2.3. Velocity

Velocity is the characteristic that tells us how fast the data is being generated in real-time. It comprises the rate at which the data is changing and linking different data sets. This velocity signifies the true capabilities of data depending on how fast the data is being processed.

Besides the three main V’s described we have three more V’s listed down recently. Veracity determines how much the data can be trusted. Value is nothing but the business value of the data and variability determines the different ways of formatting the data.

## 4\. Use of Blockchain in Big Data

The technology called Blockchain \[[26](#9781681088624-C11.html#r26)\] has been ruling for the last two decades, but it gained importance after the introduction of Bitcoins in the world. Blockchain completely focuses on distributed ledger systems, this decentralized network topology gives priority to the security systems on a higher level. Every transaction that is made *via* the consumer is stored in individual blocks. These blocks are created for storing the information and whenever a new transaction is made a new block is formed slowly this results in a chain of blocks and hence the technology is named BLOCKCHAIN. The transactions related information that is stored in the blocks must be validated by the user every time for maintaining the proper network and enabling efficient functioning. Not only the information related to the transactions but also the timestamp recording is done in a tamper-proof way. A single change that is made in the data is recorded with the proper timestamp, which is then unalterable, thereby increasing the transparency of the system. That is why blockchain is a secured system that helps in the proper preservation of data uprightness. Smart contracts \[[27](#9781681088624-C11.html#r27), [28](#9781681088624-C11.html#r28)\] are a feature of Blockchain technology, this functionality is greatly important in spontaneous resolving of the transactions in a network.

Developers concentrate on updating the information regarding each block by coding about the specifics such as payments that are present in the wallet, information about the bank accounts, and all the other details about the specifics. Once the transaction gets implemented, validation can be taken from the user who is connected to the network.

Big data is the collection of big sets of data, every data that is collected from different devices may be from sensors, networks, *etc.* Data compiled is tremendously huge and multifaceted and the processing of the traditional data. The data extracted is used for the estimation of patterns and trends that the market follows. The mechanism that big data follows is in high demand nowadays and this is because of the real-world application this technology covers underneath it. Big data is the collection of big sets of data, every data that is collected from different devices may be from sensors, networks, *etc.* Data compiled is tremendously huge and multifaceted and the processing of the traditional data. Whenever a transaction is made, it is stored in the form of ledgers also cloud services are used for recording and storing purposes but involving cloud services is a bit expensive to handle for organizations and companies. Market reactions are analyzed and calculated so that the prediction for the future market can be made easily and this is one of the major starring roles of Big data where Blockchain \[[29](#9781681088624-C11.html#r29)\] provides big support and acts as an aid.

Blockchain is an effective method for operational data storage. Not only this but the network it creates *i.e*. decentralized network is efficient in permitting admittance to multiple operators. Several parties performing transactions normally accumulate the transactional info in diverse ledgers. The parties that are involved in the transactions can be made to work on one sole network *via* Blockchain. Transactions that are taking place are recorded inside the network which is operational for the same and can be easily validated by the users of the party. As all the information is stored in the blocks formed from the blockchain hence it will help in easy access to the details stored within them. The design of the blockchain is done in such a way that it can help users to sight past transactions smoothly and can examine the trajectory of the transaction that is made.

Employing blockchain technology, Big data can be made cost-effective for corporations, as Blockchain upholds a lot of capability of storing large quantities of data and for a longer time. Corporations can elect for storage of data on a decentralized network arrangement with the usage of Blockchain. In arrears to these technologies, initiatives that will be taking place won’t be incurring costs for data storing display place. The supplementary advantage of consuming the Blockchain to grip Big Data is the solicitation of Smart Contracts. Once the essential material is collected smart contracts can accomplish transactions inevitably, thereby reducing the cost of a transaction.

The actual world example where both blockchain and big data are used simultaneously is in the banking sector in Japan. Around 50 Banks \[[26](#9781681088624-C11.html#r26), [30](#9781681088624-C11.html#r30)\] made an association and prepared an agreement with “Ripple” \[[31](#9781681088624-C11.html#r31)\]. Here, Ripple is an open tracked network that is constructed based on the technology named Blockchain. Internationally, Ripple ranked third in marketplace capitalization. This coalition arrived with the commitment to practice blockchain with big data for the menace-free and lower price transactions. It was made promising by refurbishing the outmoded Banking archetypal. The conventional archetypal had several parties that are involved and had further risk features in them. To plug in the menace and contrivance control themes, banks had to suffer by paying supplementary costs. The cost incurred because of this could have been saved by using networks of blockchain. The partnership of the Banks is also responsible for reducing the transaction time ominously.

Big Data can have a supplementary coating of security if executed on a network of blockchain. The primary layer of security can be endorsed to the network building of blockchain. Unlike the outdated approaches, the material or the content that is found in the network is protected and is not replicated. Furthermore, using Blockchain, Big Data can be easily secured, and the information can be easily organized and can be treasured. Execution and analysis of data then become more resourceful and easier. Blockchain technology is also used for fraud recognition.

### 4.1. The Motivation for use of Blockchain in Big Data

Technologies like blockchain and big data are harmonizing technologies and can completely transform the technology by introducing better storing and data exploration. Blockchain is the ruling technology of recent years, it is a database that simplifies database management and provides transparency about each record that is maintained. Blockchain makes it highly secure database technology, every record that is created in the database is known as a block and each block has details of the transaction *i.e*. made by the user. This decentralized network helps in managing and analyzing the data effectively. The record that is created includes the date of the transaction and the link that is associated with the preceding block.

Decentralization in blockchain technology is the main advantage, as no one gets the right to control the entered data, hence the integrity of the system is maintained. Continuous checks are made when the system is on any network. Every machine on the network has the same data on it, the data that is corrupted cannot come in the chain that is built. For instance let us assume the healthcare sector, wherein a hospital the data of the patients are maintained and it is ensured that the data which is collected is secured, not only this healthcare sector has to focus on updating the data related to each customer so that single detail about the health condition of the patient is missed. Now if the healthcare databases are placed on the network of blockchain then any employee can access the network easily and can update the information regarding the patient, it is also focused that the data source is kept unchangeable. Due to poor supervision of the data in the healthcare sector the risk increases, as this can lead to mishandling or poor diagnosis for the patient also if the proper track of the details related to the patient is not taken then the information related to tests can get misplaced. All sorts of risks can be easily avoided with the help of blockchain, the Fig. (**[5](#9781681088624-C11.html#f5)**) shown explains the areas that are covered to keep records.

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F5.jpg)**Fig. (5))**  
Places and fields, responsible for data tracking. 

The real motivation for using blockchain and big data together is due to the following reasons:

1.  **Better data analysis**: Data analysis is also counterparty by blockchain technology, its real-life example was seen in 2017 when 27 Japanese banks \[[32](#9781681088624-C11.html#r32)\] decided to sign up for the startup Ripple \[[31](#9781681088624-C11.html#r31)\], which simplified the money transference between the bank financial records with the assistance of blockchain. Normally the transfers take place on a real-time basis and these transfers are expensive, this is because the risk factor \[[27](#9781681088624-C11.html#r27), [33](#9781681088624-C11.html#r33)\] increases if the transactions use the identical asset. The blockchain forms a chain that helps in the easy elimination of the risk factors involved with each transaction. Real-time fraud detection can be easily made using blockchain as it will help in tracking every transaction made by the user present on the network, also permitting banks to reconnoiter data in an exploration of arrangements in actual time. Hence both the technologies together can help in strengthening the security systems in banks.
2.  **Better security:** Big data and blockchain together are also helpful in preventing data leaks in the system. When the information gets stockpiled on the system, the highest posted employee of the corporation will have to go through layers of permission from every point of the network. Thereby protects the system from every criminal interference with the data. By extension lead, the technology named Blockchain permits allotment of data with extra tranquility.
3.  **Fulfilling privacy concerns \[**[**15**](#9781681088624-C11.html#r15)**,** [**16**](#9781681088624-C11.html#r16)**,** [**34**](#9781681088624-C11.html#r34)**\]:** With the increased use of blockchain, there are several issues related to the confidentiality of the system, and this was the main reason why this technology became popular in the market. Quite a few specialists are apprehensive about the transaction accounts that may be subjugated to shape customer outlines or further misapplications. And on the involvement of blockchain, the transparency of the data gets enhanced. Just in case if the entry of data is not proved, the system will completely reject it. Hence the technology is known for data transparency.

### 4.2. Architectures of Blockchain

In the simplest form, blockchain technology is a series of timestamps \[[22](#9781681088624-C11.html#r22), [31](#9781681088624-C11.html#r31)\] creating incontrovertible registers which are then copied by the number of computers owned by different users present in the network. Each block that is created is protected and is forced to maintain cryptographic \[[35](#9781681088624-C11.html#r35)\] ideologies. The main reasons for the popularity of blockchain are:

 

*   ·A solitary object does not own the data stockpiled within the blockchain
*   ·Cryptographical \[[35](#9781681088624-C11.html#r35)\] data storage is done
*   ·Chains of blocks created are incontrovertible, hence any interference within the data is not at all allowed.
*   ·Transparency of the system is highly maintained

Blockchain was established for the manipulation and distribution of data all around the network very efficiently. A public blockchain is the famous type and Bitcoin is the part of the public blockchain. Now while the execution or implementation of a bitcoin 3 point needs to be followed:

 

*   · Who is making the transaction and from where?
*   · To whom the transaction must be sent?
*   · The money information

The block-1 of the blockchain is known as the genesis block and the previous hash block has the value 0. Fig. (**[6](#9781681088624-C11.html#f6)**) elaborates how a data block looks like.

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F6.jpg)**Fig. (6))**  
Data block. 

Every time a transaction is made or data storage has to take place a block is created and as the data keeps on increasing the number of blocks also increases concerning it hence there is a requirement of a Block header in a case like this. As data keeps on increasing the chain is formed, and for creating the chain and linking each block there is a great need for block headers. These block headers help in recognizing the sequence of each data block, also every block will have a hash of the previous block so that the linkage of each block can be easily done. The advantage of having hashes in the system is to provide immutability, *i.e*. once a block is created it cannot be changed. How the data blocks are linked to each other in the system is shown in the figure below. Each block contains data in it followed by a hash and the block number. Hash is the address of the next block, provided to maintain the linkage of each block in the system, explained in Fig. (**[7](#9781681088624-C11.html#f7)**) through the diagram.

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F7.jpg)**Fig. (7))**  
Data blocks resulting in a blockchain. 

Now block header is something that holds everything important for a data block to have, every time a transaction is made or data is stored a proper stamping of time is needed, hence the first element of a block header is a “time-stamp”. Next, a block header must include the information regarding the “version” which is the second most important thing included in a header. The third element or information is regarding the “mekle root”, which includes all the transaction history that took place. Then there is a nonce and difficulty target and a previous hash, as shown in Fig. (**[8](#9781681088624-C11.html#f8)**).

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F8.jpg)**Fig. (8))**  
Block header and mekle tree architecture. 

To make this system work efficiently, there is a requirement of a concept like “PROOF OF WORK” and the terms difficulty target and nonce are a part of this concept. Although blockchain is known for its feature of immutability what if there is a great need to add or subtract data or a block from the chain, for changing the data there will be a need to change the hash address of the block. If the data of any block is changed, it will affect the data in the next block and so on the chain formed will get disturbed. Hence, the regeneration of hashes for each block must be done which will create a problem. But as the system is created in such a way that everything is connected through a network and we are living in an age of supercomputers, hence it is not possible but how does this system become secure now, so whenever there is a change in the blockchain the concept of “PROOF OF WORK” comes into play. Now focusing on the concept of “PROOF OF WORK”, here there is a need to do some processing whenever a block must be added, and this concept includes some points such as:

 

*   · Before a change must be made, the block must be validated
*   · To add the block in a blockchain, at least 10 minutes are needed for each block
*   · Blockchain must be stored in multiple machines

Every machine in a blockchain network will have a copy of the blockchain. “Consensus Algorithm” is also used to make the changes whenever the addition of data must be made in the blockchain. For making the changes in the blockchain we need a few things like a supercomputer and a 50% network of the blockchain, which is not possible because the concept discussed in section 2.1 called “proof of work”, a large amount of CPU’s and GPU’s and affording all of this is not at all possible. Hence due to these reasons, the blockchains are kept secured. This is how the architecture of the blockchain can support data management on a larger scale.

### 4.3. Comparative Study

Big data works on three V’s are volume, velocity, and variety, which signifies that the data collected must be measured with the following terms. The data size must be defined, and it is included in the term called volume. The speed with which the data changes is defined under velocity and the data sources from where the data is collected or sent are defined by the term variety of data. Big companies like Amazon, Flipkart, *etc.* acquire millions and millions of data in a minute hence handling such a vast amount of data with frequent changes is a big challenge that is handled by big data through various platforms. Each day this technology called big data is reaching new heights and is becoming popular, it is an important tool for effective analysis of data that is currently on demand. Handling data has always been challenging for big corporations and companies hence according to their applications they developed different data platforms such as yahoo developed Zookeeper, Cassandra was developed by Facebook and many more developments like these were made. Storing big data was a challenge, hence storing and accessing the data lead to a few questions like:

 

*   · When there are multiple copies of data then how to find which data is an authenticated one?
*   · How to handle if the data gets crashed?
*   · How to monetize the data, how to know about the third-party with whom we wish to share the data?

With all these questions, the focus was to manipulate and distribute the data effectively without compromising on its security, this is when blockchain came into the picture, blockchain provided us with the platform where a secured database can be created which is immutable in nature. A block of data that is created is sequentially arranged and the database is so maintained that nobody can change the existing data. The problem of multiple copies can be easily solved by this technology as only one block is assigned for one type of data, there is no chance of data to get lost in the system as the hashes follow the data blocks very strictly and the block header helps in a systematic arrangement of the processing that will take place with each transaction.

On noticing the later technology of blockchain \[[13](#9781681088624-C11.html#r13)^(\-)[16](#9781681088624-C11.html#r16)\], researchers thought of adding the former and the latter together for building the better system and this new technology was named “BIGCHAINDB”. The advantages of this technology are that the infrastructure can easily be spread across various locations and the properties of the database, such as integrity and consistency, are also maintained simultaneously.

Blockchain DB works on the data pipeline which includes every step from data collection to data management. The steps are as follows:

 

*   · Data is collected *via* IOT sensors
*   · kinesis/event hub
*   · Stream analysis
*   · HFDS storage
*   · Spark data cleaning
*   · Spark normalization
*   · MongoDB storage
*   · Tableau analysis

Before the data pipeline step, time stamping of the input data is done which is as follows:

 

*   · Create a transaction (a hash of the data and metadata)
*   · Cryptographically sign the transaction
*   · Write the transaction to the blockchain database, this will automatically timestamp the transaction.

BigchainDB \[[36](#9781681088624-C11.html#r36), [37](#9781681088624-C11.html#r37)\] has features like decentralization, immutability, low latency, customizable, rich per missioning.

Blockchain and big data when combined work on three key parts:

 

*   · A computer networks
*   · A network protocols
*   · Consensus mechanism

Each computer in the system copies the ledger and single changes are made in the algorithm to ensure that the changes made are valid. Whatever gets approved is added to the chain data. Many companies are creating their private blockchain because this will help them eliminate the chances of interference, saving both time and money, thereby increasing the safety and credibility of the network.

### 4.4. Challenges and Solutions

As per the title, the main target is to find how blockchain can help in the efficient management of big data which is the fastest rising segment. In every single business model, the collection of accurate insights is the most important point, collecting insights and then analyzing the use patterns according to the customers' demand. While talking about big data, data sets become complementary. Big data \[[38](#9781681088624-C11.html#r38)\] is a technology that deals with the huge set of datasets that are analyzed and the patterns are revealed accordingly using progressive statistical representations and data mining. But at the same time, this technology has some challenges to work upon, which are discussed as follows:

 

#### 4.4.1. Data Management

Due to the production of massive amounts of the day each minute in every possible sector, data management is the biggest challenge for companies. They find it difficult to manage the huge amount of data as a large volume of data is present and organizing this data properly and then finally concluding from the organized data is a tedious task. Not only management of the data is focused but retaining the data fetched is also necessary. The collected data is highly versatile in nature, it can be unstructured as well as structured data, hence classifying each type and drawing conclusions will allow corporations to collect insights from the market demands and then they can effectively make changes in their respective products. Hence, overcoming this challenge is extremely important for which blockchain can be used, as blockchain \[[39](#9781681088624-C11.html#r39)\] provides well-defined blocks that can not only retain data but every input data is timestamped and is completely immutable.

 

#### 4.4.2. Risks

When a corporation has a large amount of data to analyze and if in case it mistakenly misinterprets the data fetched then this can create risks for different levels because a flawed result can lead to a great loss which can affect both the company and the market. Hence, understanding the data efficiently is very important. To build efficient models for better prediction, corporations must understand data and how it follows the complex relationship \[[40](#9781681088624-C11.html#r40)\] with the system. Blockchain can also help in reducing the risk factor as it is the systematic technology focusing on how to easily analyze the data and store it in the system, hence blockchain solutions can increase the efficacy when combined with big data.

 

#### 4.4.3. Data Security

Data stealing is an increasing region of misconduct, and according to the recent surveys it has been seen that the more data a system has, there will be more chances of hackers attacking it. And as each company deals with its customers and must provide security for the information shared, threats like these should be completely prevented. For data protection, the use of blockchain can be made, being a decentralized network, it will allow data to seep in but is highly secured and immutable, hence will protect data from all kinds of threats and will ensure data privacy.

## 5\. Applications

The various applications are discussed in this section.

### 5.1. Healthcare

When discussing blockchain and its applications, most people tend to think about bitcoins and other financial applications. Strangely, some of the most promising future applications for blockchain are presented in more than two fields. Healthcare is one such field presenting varied and useful blockchain applications that can potentially change the field. The applications vary widely from relief efforts to tracking pharmaceutical trials but blockchains decentralizing and the democratic principles remain key to the efforts, as elaborated in Fig. (**[9](#9781681088624-C11.html#f9)**). There are ten major blockchain applications in healthcare \[[41](#9781681088624-C11.html#r41)\].

Despite offering many areas of improvement in the field of healthcare, the most promising application is perhaps Electronic Health Records/Electronic Medical Records (EMR) \[[42](#9781681088624-C11.html#r42)\]. EMR is a medical record maintained by the health provider containing information such as diagnosis, prognosis, and lab test results. Maintaining the integrity of this data is of the utmost importance while maintaining the patient’s privacy. Blockchain provides an elegant solution for the same using its distributed ledger technology. Using blockchain will enhance security while at the same time provide easy access to medical records.

### 5.2. Education

Blockchain has the potential to systematically transform the education system with its technology. Its applications are extensive, ranging from infrastructural improvements to smart contracts. Blockchains used in educational institutions cannot be underestimated considering that this sector deals with big data in a variety of formats. Blockchain technology can also successfully link up validators, educators, students, and employers all in one system, thereby making all actions seamless and more efficient. Verifying transcripts and other important documents is usually a labor-heavy task involving a lot of documentation. With the advent of blockchain technology verification of these transcripts can be made a click process. The technology can also be used to store student directories and will provide a lot of added benefits considering its distributed ledger technology. Transportation issues can also be solved by employing blockchain-based startups such as ‘Arcade City’ \[[43](#9781681088624-C11.html#r43)\]. Cloud storage is also a potential area of improvement using technology.

![](Images/9781681088624-C11_F9.jpg)**Fig. (9))**  
Blockchain applications in healthcare. 

Smart contracts can revolutionize many industries in the world, including education \[[44](#9781681088624-C11.html#r44)\]. Once certain conditions are met, it can affect educational institutions and make them more efficient by reducing documentation and executing agreements. Scholarships and funding transactions will become hassle-free if smart contracts are implemented. Financial aid will only be received by the student in case the required coursework is complete.

### 5.3. Finance

The financial industry is probably the largest and most data-intensive industry in the market. It is giant, deals with tremendous amounts of data, and has speed as a necessity in all transactions. When it comes to big data, blockchain, AI is one of the first adopters and experimenters of possibilities in the financial industry. Efficiency is a critical matter, and blockchain allows the way for the transaction of these funds globally. Ledgers have changed the financial industry that’s one of the reasons that this sector is taken up by the blockchain. Consumer expectations are changing, they want a better experience, they want their needs to be matched, that's when AI is also used. This helps in offering better services and it can be done at a much faster pace. The financial industry is producing lots and lots of data every single second with the transaction that is made. Several factors play an important role when it comes to the financial industry. Our financial lives are changing the acceleration of digital transformation, customer and data-driven financial services are key to these changes transforming financial services with blockchain and big data. When the customer’s integrated authentication information is registered with the digital identity, it is lately propagated throughout blockchain enabling convenient and secure user authentication for a wide variety of services. Using blockchain-based digital points, users can make app-to-app payments, can transfer these points overseas in real-time, and can collect those points *via* ATM, because all these transactions are enabled through blockchain with increased limits and safety. Integration of scattered customer financial information such as spending, investments, and assets can now be shown in an app and financial information for analysis is de-identified and is managed securely through Blockchain. It also has AI virtual assistant which can recommend the virtual product and customers can register their recommended product at once. Next, the digitalization of finance also changes the financial environment, AI virtual assistant allows financial institutions to provide faster customer services. It also makes it easy for customers to submit documents, and while making insurance claims customers can authenticate themselves easily, and the data gets directly transferred from one place to another through blockchain.

### 5.4. IoT

As we move into the world of connectivity and networks, a new feature in technology is introduced. The technology present at the time of the industrial age was machined, people at that time were trying to put their hands into the mechanization of systems so that the information from the environment can be recorded efficiently by slowly moving towards how to efficiently manage every single connection between the machines. Presently, almost every device is connected through the network.

The emphasis is on cooperating IoT \[[45](#9781681088624-C11.html#r45)\] and blockchain \[[46](#9781681088624-C11.html#r46), [47](#9781681088624-C11.html#r47)\] and fusing them to form a new technology named BioT. This technology will help the customer by providing a decentralized network that collaborates with IoT and can help in resolving the issues by adopting peer to peer network. Millions of data are generated and are shared between the devices which lower down the cost and assign computational and storage requirements crossways several diplomacies, thereby, preventing the network from failure.

When peer to peer network becomes customary, several defies are introduced related to the security of the system, IoT when focuses the aspect of security \[[48](#9781681088624-C11.html#r48)\], does not only focuses on protecting the sensitive data but includes many other things in it. When blockchain and IoT get combined, they lead to a solution that can easily maintain the privacy of the network, this is done by asking for validation each time the data is accessed by an individual, the agreement has to pass for processing each transaction, this can help in avoiding deceiving and robbery.

## CONCLUSION

Blockchain is in its emerging phase and has got hyped in a very short time. When the technology will develop further, more tangible use cases can be acknowledged and discovered, and big data management will become easier with the involvement of blockchain in it. Large amounts of data with massive datasets must be handled properly as storing this data, its manipulation, and its distribution are the major challenges for handling such large amounts of data. All these problems related to big data can be solved using blockchain.

The only apprehension is that blockchain presentation in this respect will be exclusive to chase, which is due to data storing *i.e*. done on the block. Blocks present in blockchain deal with comparatively minor quantities of data equated to the huge capacities of data composed every second for the technologies like big data. But it is dealt with intuitively by creating a chain of blocks containing the actual data, blockchain thereby provides the platform for handling such large amounts of data efficiently.

# Blockchain Security Solutions for IoT and Big Data

  
  
**H. Abhijith^([1](#9781681088624-C12.html#aff1), [\*](#9781681088624-C12.html#cor1))****,** **Deepti Gupta^([1](#9781681088624-C12.html#aff1))****,** **Shilpi Sharma^([1](#9781681088624-C12.html#aff1))**  
¹ Amity School of Engineering and Technology, Amity University, Noida, U.P., India  
  

## Abstract

Technologies shaping the future, Internet of Things (IoT) and Big Data, are the leading research topics in the computer science department. Every field like this is prone to attacks from hackers and adversaries, which is why there is a need for proper security method implementations. The current nature of the Internet of Things uses centralized servers for data collection and analysis requirements, which invites many security concerns. Similarly, big data deals with a huge amount of data that is processed and analyzed every day. The blockchain network can fill this need for a better security solution. Even though it is in its infancy but due to the features, such as Decentralization, Persistency, Anonymity, and Auditability, it is able to strengthen the security of the various IoT and big data applications. This paper discusses the security flaws in the existing centralized infrastructure used by the above technologies and presents the security solution to solve the flaws.

**Keywords:** Big data, Blockchain, Internet of Things, Security solution.  
  

* * *

^(\*) **Corresponding author H. Abhijith:** Amity School of Engineering and Technology, Amity University, Noida, U.P., India; E-mail: [abhijithrajihari@gmail.com](mailto:abhijithrajihari@gmail.com)  
  
 

## 1\. INTRODUCTION

The Internet of Things (IoT) is a collection of interconnected computing devices, mechanical and digital computers, objects, animals, or unique identifiers (UIDs) that can transmit information over a network from one person or without the need for human-computer interaction. However, it comes with many security issues that have many similarities with the security of IT \[[1](#9781681088624-C12.html#r1)\]. Many current IoT networks rely on centralized networking modes for connecting to servers or cloud services for data processing and storage. The fear is that the server will become a bottleneck and a new target for cyber-attack, as well as a failure point that will disrupt the whole network and harm the consistency of the tests. Consequently, it remains complicated how to create a fully reliable and interconnected ecosystem to support such embedded devices and computer resources for data transmission \[[1](#9781681088624-C12.html#r1)\].

Big data is a concept describing the vast amount of data that inundates an enterprise on a regular basis- both organized and unstructured.

However, what is relevant is not the volume of information but what the organizations do with the said critical information. Some users use this info for their own gain and do selfish mining or share private information with companies. So, to overcome these issues with IoT and Big data, blockchain is implemented \[[2](#9781681088624-C12.html#r2)\]. Blockchain is a decentralized system, which removes the use of a third party in a transaction thereby creating a peer-to-peer connection. It is based on functionalities like encryption, decentralization, immutability, and transparency. All the data stored in a blockchain is coupled in blocks like a database. All the transactions are connected with nodes. When a new transaction appears, the sender via a peer-to-peer network communicates it to all the different nodes. As the transactions are being validated, it is stored in the transactional pool. When the transactions are being validated in the pool, miners create a new block and store all the transactions thereafter which the block is mined \[[2](#9781681088624-C12.html#r2)\].

This paper discusses the security flaws associated with IoT and Big data. It also tells about the possible security problems that are associated while implementing blockchain. This paper also gives some possible solutions to these problems. There are various examples to elaborate the idea of implementing and its various outcomes in this paper. We review some of the alternatives given by other papers like cloud-based drone systems for data collection \[[3](#9781681088624-C12.html#r3)\] and Fair Access \[[4](#9781681088624-C12.html#r4)\]. The paper focuses on the security solutions based on blockchain designed for an IoT network. These approaches include the security maintenance of IoT computers, safe firmware upgrades, and trust verification of a trustworthy computer base, authentication of IoT device identity, and secure data store access to control information systems. The paper also focuses on a Blockchain-based IoT Device Security Architecture in Smart Homes where the architecture consists of three levels, namely local smart home networks, an overlay network, and cloud storage \[[1](#9781681088624-C12.html#r1)\]. Entities use blockchain transfers for coordination between themselves in each stage. This paper reflects on the IoT blockchain platform by analyzing future data interruptions and security issues during the interaction with IoT components. These security issues include confidentiality of data, lack of resources for design practices, inadequate information on upgrade and maintenance \[[5](#9781681088624-C12.html#r5)\].

The paper is organized as follows:

Section II presents the literature related to the topic. Section III analyzes and discusses various security issues faced by IoT and big data, followed by a review of the proposed and implemented security solution of blockchain. Section IV discusses the growth of the security issues discussed in the previous section and how there is a need for a blockchain solution. Section V summarizes the related work. Section VI concludes the paper with an outline of open issues and future work.

 

## 2\. LITERATURE REVIEW

In this section, we will discuss the various conclusion derived from the papers read as part of the background study.

A study conducted by \[[6](#9781681088624-C12.html#r6)\], Zheng *et al.* raises various privacy issues and security threats like lack of optimally controlled role in communication between the devices in the IoT to prevent hijacking and cyberattacks, fairness in data collection and use, and identification in IoT. These challenges can be solved by blockchain as it enables a decentralized or a distributed network. However, it also brings out challenges like a proper ledger storage facility, severe inadequacy of appropriate legal standards, and limited research in the technology. Karafiloski *et al.* \[[7](#9781681088624-C12.html#r7)\] discuss some major characteristics that build up a blockchain network such as auditability, anonymity, persistency, and decentralization. These characteristics help to build a secure network, which can be used for Big Data and IoT. A proper consensus algorithm is key to a successful blockchain network. An efficient consensus algorithm provides safety and convenience. There are also the same challenges that occur while using a blockchain network some of which are scalability and privacy leakage (Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#t1)).
