According to Thakore *et al.* \[[8](#9781681088624-C12.html#r8)\], blockchain is a distributed ledger accessible to the public that stores all the executed transactions in the system. The way forward in big data using blockchain is combining blockchain with smart contracts. Blockchain combined with smart contracts can contribute to inventing the next era of application with a more established security and verification system. When users build and post data, they will also remain primary data owners. With respect to the surveillance that is achieved by recording the behavior and desires of users, users should at least have knowledge of it and any advantages. The Blockchain can be a proxy for privacy control rights or a completely autonomous social network can be introduced. Blockchain provides user control of all data and transactions they make. While preventing any checks from third parties. This definition may affect Big Data in seeking a solution for the distributed storage and management of data on a P2P network.

Xu *et al.*concluded \[[9](#9781681088624-C12.html#r9)\] that blockchain overcomes the need for a third-party arbitrator on a centralized network for coordinating transactions among entities (Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#t1)). Blockchain bases its implementation on four main ideas; mining, distributed public ledger, ledger copies Synchronization, and a peer-to-peer network. Merging blockchain and IoT helps support peer-to-peer communication enabling autonomy and thus removing all the bottlenecks that would have occurred otherwise. IoT and blockchain mergers can be viable solutions for both economic efficiency and business needs. Blockchain is always not a good idea for an architecture base it depends on the type of application, which demands the use of features of the blockchain.

A blockchain network is as good as its consensus algorithm; according to Reyna *et al*., \[[10](#9781681088624-C12.html#r10)\], we see a green proof of collaboration consensus mechanism. The key concept of proof of collaboration is that participants contribute to the sharing of big data so that they can also benefit from the collaboration of other participants. While blockchain brings so many exciting features such as decentralization and immutability, the restricted computing resources, network, and computer storage resources present challenges to the blockchain architecture.

Li *et al.* \[[11](#9781681088624-C12.html#r11)\] suggested (Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#t1)) that in an IoT network, any non-trustworthy part of the network can tamper with the information provided in line with their own interests, thus feeding unreliable information in the system. To solve this, we have to implement a distributed network using blockchain, which is trusted by all its participants that guarantees the immutability of the data. Blockchain provides the decentralization and security required for a secure IoT. Integration of blockchain improves the following; Decentralization, Market of services, Identity, Autonomy, scalability, Reliability, Security, and Secure code deployment. Even though Blockchain is a good alternative to the centralized cloud-based data collection network, there are still some challenges like Scalability, Storage capacity, Legal issues, *etc.*

Dorri *et al.* \[[12](#9781681088624-C12.html#r12)\] concentrated (Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#t1)) on the vulnerability in the security of the blockchain technology by researching the existing blockchain systems like Ethereum, Bitcoin, *etc.* They discuss the 51% vulnerability where the consensus mechanism itself has 51% vulnerability, and then the adversaries exploit this vulnerability to take control of the blockchain network and other problems like double spending, transaction privacy leakage, and private key security. All these problems existed in blockchain 1.0, there are some problems specific to blockchain 2.0 where the use of smart contracts (A smart contract can be considered as a lightweight dAPP (decentralized application) are enabled, like Vulnerabilities in a smart contract, Criminal smart contracts, Under-optimized smart contract, Under-priced operations. The paper also discusses enhancements to be made on the blockchain network; one of the enhancements is the implementation of a smart pool.

In a study \[[13](#9781681088624-C12.html#r13)\], Alphand *et al.* suggested (Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#t1)) a new, blockchain-based vehicle safety architecture. BC inherits the anonymity of the current system, where each node uses a special private key that communicates with other overlay nodes. So, an overlay node protects overlay nodes from being registered. To store data that is subject to the privacy, each vehicle is fitted with in-vehicle storage. The architecture supports evolving automotive technologies by offering a safe way to exchange data while offering end-user security. The focus of the proposed architecture called Lightweight Scalable Blockchain is to create a decentralized privacy-preserving and secure blockchain-based architecture for the smart vehicles, which have a low overhead. The paper also discussed various applications pertaining to the proposed architecture from car-sharing services to smart charging services.

Puthal *et al.* \[[14](#9781681088624-C12.html#r14)\] suggested (Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#t1)) an IoTChain architecture that combines the elements of OSCAR (Object Protection Infrastructure for the Internet of Things) and the ACE authorization system. IoTChain architecture uses a single ACE authorization server blockchain. The said blockchain handles demands for authorization by smart contracts. In addition, a primary distributing scheme for the self-healing community is used for the effective multicasting of IoT resources. The self-healing group key distribution allows the creation of group keys for dynamic groups of large numbers of users to secure multicast communication over an unreliable network.

Banerjee *et al.* \[[15](#9781681088624-C12.html#r15)\] indicates that the blockchain network can only be introduced if it is relevant and allows for improved benefit protection. The agreement is confirmed by the participants by means of predefined processes of authentication and consensus, without any intervention from a central third-party program. Owing to its heavily secure nature achieved with sophisticated cryptography, the information contained in the global public ledger is resistant to any tampering. And the system seeks cybersecurity uses. As centralized systems are getting rid of in the IoT, so mobile devices can upgrade applications, fix glitches, and talk directly. The blockchain can be a successful answer for the century’s old consensus problem. However, blockchain cannot be expected to solve everything related to verification.

Kshetri *et al.* \[[16](#9781681088624-C12.html#r16)\] classify (Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#t1)) the different intrusion prevention and detection techniques by approaches, network structures, applications, *etc.* It also delves into different collaborative security techniques. The paper also focuses on the need for publicly accessible datasets, as the position of real-world datasets in evaluating the proposed protection strategy cannot be overestimated, especially predictive. The need for safe storage of publicly accessible IoT databases in order to establish a specification for these databases using blockchain technology in order to maintain transparency of shared data. The future of the blockchain can be seen in both civilian and military contexts, including the drone internet, the Battlefield internet, and the Military things internet. Therefore, Blockchain becomes all the more important for providing a secure decentralized network architecture.

Pulkkis *et al.* suggested that \[[17](#9781681088624-C12.html#r17)\] (Table [**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#t1)) IoT devices have the ability to use smart contracts to execute autonomous transactions. IoT computers are vulnerable to malware, DDoS attacks, remote hijacking, and data theft attacks. When any of the systems connecting to the IoT network was infected, the whole network and the associated system may also be affected. In the blockchain, it is impossible to alter a transaction that has already been accepted to make untrustworthy devices connected to the network by feeding fake signatures into a framework. There's no single point of failure or weakness in blockchain except for the time-stamping clock necessary.

Zheng *et al.* \[[18](#9781681088624-C12.html#r18)\] focuses on the already existing and proposed security solutions based on blockchain designed for an IoT network. Security solutions include secure management of IoT devices, secure firmware updates, trust assessment of a trusted computing base, validation of IoT device identity, secure data storage system for access control information. This also focuses on a Blockchain-based IoT System Protection Infrastructure in Smart Homes in which the system has three layers, including local smart home networks, an overlay network, and cloud storage. Entities use blockchain transfers for coordination between themselves in each stage. The categories of transactions include genesis transactions, storage transactions, control transactions, and transaction tracking. They also put emphasis on the use of IoT devices in the field of medicine where a proposed plan is reviewed.

Dorri *et al.* suggested that \[[19](#9781681088624-C12.html#r19)\] Blockchain can transform the traditional industry by its various characteristics namely anonymity, decentralization, *etc.* This technology can be applied to several fields other than bitcoin too but right now, it is guarded by it. Many smart contract applications have been designed to overcome the difficulties and problems faced by Blockchain. However, this paper does not give any solution for problems like selfish mining. There is no way proposed to detect the trading patterns of the users so that it can be used to suggest feasible partners, according to their pattern. Smart contract languages have many limits and defects in them.

Khan *et al.* \[[20](#9781681088624-C12.html#r20)\] suggested that blockchain is known for its privacy and security. An optimized blockchain is introduced here so that the overhead present in the typical blockchain can be eliminated while retaining its benefits. This blockchain constitutes a hierarchical architecture that uses a centralized private Immutable Ledger at the local end so that overhead can be eliminated and uses a decentralized public blockchain at higher-end devices for the trust of the users. Although, the example used in this paper *i.e.* the scenario of a smart home has application agnostic architecture for diverse IoT use cases. No extensive evaluation on the impact of design on security and overhead generated in the overlay. No comprehensive analysis on security and consensus of the framework.

In a study \[[21](#9781681088624-C12.html#r21)\], Minoli *et al.* addressed security issues of IoT, and possible solutions have been given including implementing Blockchain. These issues are created because of the immature standards of IoT and also due to the resources that are constrained. IoT does not have secure hardware and software deployment, design, and development. This paper summarizes many open challenges like single points of failure, blockchain vulnerabilities but does not provide any solution for the same. The paper does not give any survey results or graphs to evaluate the solutions.

Atlam *et al.* \[[22](#9781681088624-C12.html#r22)\] conclude that blockchain is used in the application layer and at the lower layer, which makes it more convenient to use across the layers and enables the synergetic use of the mechanism. Blockchains are embedded with various security mechanisms like encryption, firewalling, *etc.* to facilitate IoT integrity. However, not all the problems can be resolved using blockchain mechanisms because it is not practical. Those systems entail nodes with the appropriate capabilities to support the P2P functionality.

According to Ouaddah *et al.*, \[[23](#9781681088624-C12.html#r23)\], IoT is nowadays used everywhere in our day-to- day life. However, the client/server model associated has many issues like security and scalability. To overcome these issues blockchain is implemented as it provides distributed peer-to-peer interaction where no involvement of a third party is required and trust is not breached. This paper also shows that although with the implementation of blockchain many issues have already been resolved, new challenges are continuously emerging that need to be addressed.

Casino *et al.* \[[24](#9781681088624-C12.html#r24)\] has introduced a new blockchain applicability area, which is access control by access control framework Fair Access. Bitcoin-like blockchain-based cryptocurrency offers consistency and this framework leverages it to provide a transparent and stronger access control tool. This paper has used UML to provide us with a dynamic and static description of the framework. A detailed technical application is mentioned along with the initial execution and implementation using Raspberry Pi and a typical IoT use case.

Rabah *et al.* \[[25](#9781681088624-C12.html#r25)\] highlight all the features of blockchain and shows how it is ideal for many applications. Blockchain should not replace every database system there is as there are some places where the traditional methods should be used instead of blockchain, as it is more beneficial. There is a survey conducted to show many of the industries and domains using blockchains. Individual characteristics of blockchain are shown so that appropriate blockchain can be selected for the required purpose.

Liang *et al.* \[[26](#9781681088624-C12.html#r26)\] provide detailed information about big data, IoT, blockchain, and AI. Big data can facilitate services for both public and private services. It carefully assembles data and tells which data should be shared and which data to be kept private. This can benefit many organizations and industries. This paper shows a similarity between big data, IoT, blockchain, and AI and how they are dependent on each other. This mutual relationship makes them the ideal alternative to every problem.

The deduction derived from the paper by Liang *et al.* \[[27](#9781681088624-C12.html#r27)\] is that there are many areas of cyber-attacks increasing day by day in IoT applications. The advancement of this technology gives rise to many threats. This paper focuses on removing those threats using blockchain. To exemplify this idea, they have given a cloud-based drone system for the collection of data. This paper proposes a general architecture of a drone data collection system using blockchain. This system ensures reliability and reduces the number of data loss and risks for real-time data collection using a drone system. A prototype has been evaluated for the drone system to contemplate its performance.

Yue *et al.* \[[28](#9781681088624-C12.html#r28)\] discuss ProvChain, a data provenance infrastructure for ensuring data operations in a cloud storage environment while improving security and availability at the same time. ProvChain stores the project history as provenance data to be hashed into the nodes of the Merkle tree. Blockchain-based data provenance can have credibility and reliability by exploiting the blockchain network 's global computational capacity. The hashed identity of users is retained in the given architecture in order to protect their anonymity from the rest of the nodes in a blockchain network.

In the paper by Zamora *et al.* \[[29](#9781681088624-C12.html#r29)\], blockchain technologies are incorporated to address some of the important issues like exploitation of personal data and storage, incorporating blockchain and smart contracts to create a decentralized model of data exchange without intrusive third parties, splitting the existing “island” network, and increasing data reputation. For the data supplier, the decentralized architecture eliminates the threats to data protection posed by over-centralization to data storage; as for customers, the blockchain operating model guarantees consistency in the knowledge collection phase and protects the privacy of users and the right to learn.

 

## 3\. METHODOLOGY

In this section, the methodology of investigating the rising threat of two of the major problems namely ransomware and botnet faced by IoT and big data is introduced. After discussing the security risks, security solutions are also being introduced.

The data source for both ransomware and botnet detection were collected over the year 2019, *i.e.* from January 1 through December 31. Data collected for ransomware particularly ransomware ryuk was from the customers of Malwarebytes \[[30](#9781681088624-C12.html#r30)\] from the accounts that are active so as to reduce any irregularities in the data. After the collection of the data, we plot a graph showing the detection of the ransomware ryuk by each month. Data of botnets detected were collected using the “follow the spam” \[[31](#9781681088624-C12.html#r31)\] method and then a graph was plotted showing the detected botnets by nine countries that had the greatest number of botnets detected over the year 2019.

 

### 3.1. Security Challenges

 

#### 3.1.1. Security Challenges in IoT

**Botnet Attacks** A botnet is a network of multiple internets connected devices, where every device is running one or more than one bot and are in control by the attacker. They are used to perform various types of illicit attacks, one of the main being Distributed Denial-of-Service (DDoS) attacks as the adversary has full access to the affected devices \[[32](#9781681088624-C12.html#r32)\]. It becomes more dangerous as a user may be unknown if their device is part of a botnet. Botnets are made by attacking the weak point of a device in the IoT network and exposing its vulnerability. After the vulnerability is exposed, the attacker gains access to the device and continues to affect the other devices in the network as they are in a centralized network creating a botnet. One of the most famous and known examples of a botnet is the Mirai botnet. Mirai is a malware that has infiltrated IoT devices and co-opted them into a botnet for DDoS. By continuously propagating to weakly designed IoT devices \[[33](#9781681088624-C12.html#r33)\], Mirai triggers a DDoS against a group of target servers at its height, Mirai corrupted and co-opted nearly 600k devices \[[34](#9781681088624-C12.html#r34)\]. The botnet is also evolving as most of them have a distributed architecture which makes it difficult to trace the attackers down \[[35](#9781681088624-C12.html#r35)\].

**Ransomware Attacks** Ransomware is the most common type of attack performed on any platform. It is a subset of malware and only has a single purpose to encrypt the user’s data to lock them out of their own files, and then the attacker demands a ransom for the removal of the said malware \[[36](#9781681088624-C12.html#r36)\]. In terms of IoT, the ransomware attack could be more than just locking out the victim but could jeopardize confidentiality, integrity, and availability \[[37](#9781681088624-C12.html#r37)\]. The Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3) reported a loss of around $8.9 million in the year 2019 alone. One example is the WannaCry / WannaDecryptor; it is a form of ransomware called crypto-ransomware that applies encryption and decryption algorithms to computer data, and it is riskier if it targets back-end application servers because IoT devices at the forefront do not contain a significant amount of data \[[37](#9781681088624-C12.html#r37)\].

**Data Integrity** IoT in this new era is everywhere and some estimates show that by 2025, the world will have approximately 21 billion IoT devices \[[38](#9781681088624-C12.html#r38)\]. This leads to a huge level of sensitive data collected by the sensors and actuators embedded or connected with these devices. Hence, it is important the integrity of this data is protected and authentic. This is where the challenges arise *i.e.* to maintain the data integrity. It becomes all the more important as some of the sensors in the IoT network have high data collection rates for bandwidth and low latency (real-time). Suitable techniques for data integrity such as encryption and watermarking are therefore required \[[39](#9781681088624-C12.html#r39)\].

**Authentication** Whenever a device is used, it always performs an authentication check in order to verify the user trying to access the device has the appropriate permission to do so. An IoT network consists of sensors, actuators, internet gateways, Edge IT, and a data center each having its unique functionalities to perform certain operations. In an IoT network, each of these components has to be properly authenticated to make the network safe to implement. There is already existing infrastructure for this purpose in IoT acknowledged as Public Key Infrastructure (PKI) where digital certificates are used to provide the authenticity proof of the IoT device but the use of blockchain’s use of distributed ledger could enhance PKI and ensure digital certificate management is auditable and any changes made are irreversible \[[40](#9781681088624-C12.html#r40)\].

 

#### 3.1.2. Security Challenges in Big Data

**Data Growth.** Big data deals with an enormous amount of data and it keeps increasing every day. Today, everybody uses a centralized data center for this flow of data. By the end of 2020, the total amount will be sufficient to fill a stack of tablets that reach 6.6 times from Earth to Moon \[[41](#9781681088624-C12.html#r41)\]. YouTube the media-sharing platform single-handedly generates so much data that they have a server with the capacity of one Exabyte (one billion gigabytes) as every minute 300 hours of video is uploaded on the platform \[[42](#9781681088624-C12.html#r42)\]. This ever-growing scale of data with much of the data being data unstructured needs to be put in check so that the centralized data center does not overflow.

**Ownership and Monetization.** Digital Proprietary deals with the ownership of digital data that can be an artwork in the form of audio, video, or text. It is very important that the author or creator of an artwork receive proper credit for their work. Currently, if a creator puts their artwork onto a platform there is no way to protect the authenticity of that artwork as people can impose as the original creator of the artwork and take credit for it. Creators do not even have a proper channel to be fairly paid for their work \[[7](#9781681088624-C12.html#r7)\].

**Data Modification.** The authenticity of the data is of utmost importance as it is analyzed and processed and correct data means correct analytical results. Data modification makes this process difficult as the data could be modified and generate incorrect insights. Similar to data modification, data validation is also a challenge. Sometimes organizations doing the analytical research receive the same fragment of data from different systems, which does not always agree with the records on those different systems. For example, an electronic health record (EHR) program in a hospital might have a prescription of medication separate from that of its partner pharmacy \[[41](#9781681088624-C12.html#r41)\].

**Data Security.** The most crucial challenge is protecting the data from adversaries who may try to mine sensitive information. Data security not only means to secure the data from stealing but also means to secure the data from possibly being false. Cybercriminals may try to do so by generating fake sensor data and thus the report will be incorrect. Organizations using big data research to sell their products aggregate their data in a single central data server, storing both structured and unstructured data. It becomes a valuable target for attackers by centralizing data in one place, which can potentially leave huge amounts of information exposed, which could undermine organizational trust and damage its reputation \[[43](#9781681088624-C12.html#r43)\].

 

### 3.2. Blockchain Solutions

 

#### 3.2.1. Security Solutions for IoT

**AutoBotCatcher**\- Gokhan *et al.* \[[5](#9781681088624-C12.html#r5)\] proposed a blockchain solution to catch the malicious botnets incurring illicit activities. They proposed an Auto Bot Catcher, which uses a Byzantine Fault Tolerance (BFT) blockchain in a permissioned setting that detects the bots by processing the network flow data. The use of BFT is explained by its characteristics of assigning the role of generating block to a small set of actors. Using the blockchain in a permitted setting helps to keep adversaries' stored network data confidential, as only a certain set of pre-identified block generators can access the above data. BFT approach follows the two-thirds consensus protocol, *i.e.* to reach consensus on the current set of rules introduced by one of the block generators, which at least two-thirds of the pre-identified block generators have to decide upon. The set of rules is the same mutual contact graph, set of blocks processed, and community mapping of IoT devices within this proposed mechanism. The AutoBotCatcher is comprised of two entities Agents, who monitor the network traffic flow, and block generators who as the name suggests, are part of the consensus and block generation process.

As shown in Fig. ([**1**](#9781681088624-C12.html#f1)), AutoBotCatcher processes the safely stored network flow data of IoT devices and then generates a mutual contact graph after the pre-processing of determining whether the IP address belongs to the *blacklist* and *whitelist* maintained by agents. If a blacklisted IP address is detected, then agents sever the network connection or force it to shut down essentially quarantining it. After the generation of the mutual contact graph, it is analyzed by performing Dynamic Community Discovery (DCD) using the Louvain method \[[22](#9781681088624-C12.html#r22)\] but by giving it, the community set of IoT devices from the previous state. Then it performs two checks perturbation check and botnet check for old and new communities respectively. A perturbation check is performed to add or remove the IP addresses from the blacklist based on them being a part of a botnet managed by the block manager. Whereas, botnet check classifies the new communities either as a botnet community or as a safe community also managed by the block manager. The last stage consists of the block manager updating the blacklist from the identified bots and simultaneously updating the blacklist of all IoT devices using a smart contract.

![](Images/9781681088624-C12_F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
System Flow of AutoBotCatcher. 

**Framework for Prevention of Ransomware Attack.** Kaur *et al.* in a study \[[44](#9781681088624-C12.html#r44)\] proposed a framework to prevent ransomware attacks by using the blockchains smart contract feature. In the framework, there are regular gateway checks for malicious ports or IP addresses. However, if any advanced malware breaks through then the blockchain smart contract check three main things: System Calls; Authenticity; Access Control. If the software behaves normally like read/write file or query a file then it is deemed as a normal user behavior but the access of sensitive information like network information or kernel level information, the user is alerted of malicious software in the system. The user then gives it appropriate permission to execute if it is non-malicious software. The flagged aforementioned software may try to make malicious system calls to encrypt or gain control of the desktop then the blockchain will stop the process in all the devices except both the honeypot devices if it safely runs on those devices and no ransom calls are made the software is deemed as non-malicious. However, if a ransom call is made then it is flagged as malicious software, and the software is uninstalled from the device, and honeypot devices are updated with new rules. This framework also supports a backup feature, which uses the blockchain cloud infrastructure.

**Sensing data integrity.** In 2019, Hang *et al.* \[[45](#9781681088624-C12.html#r45)\] implemented blockchain to make an IoT-based Blockchain Platform. Similar to the previously discussed solutions, this is also heavily based on the use of smart contracts. The smart contracts mentioned in the paper contains five fields: Device owner registration function; Device registration function; Sensor reading function; Actuator Writing Function; Event Notification. This smart contract is connected to the ledger, which receives ledger updates and sends back update results. The ledger and the smart contract make the IoT-Blockchain network and is connected to the application client, which executes the smart contract and receives ledger updates. When a new device is added to the network, the client registers the device invoking the device registration transaction mentioned in the aforementioned smart contract. After that consensus, a process is held and each peer updates his or her blockchain with this new transaction followed by the client being informed of the transaction’s execution. When the device is successfully registered, the device owner can deploy a task to be done by a particular device. The targeted device after executing the task sends the result to both the server and blockchain. The blockchain then updates the distributed ledger of each peer and sends the result to the device. In addition, alerting the device owner if the pre-defined conditions are satisfied, for example, humidity level of the air reaches an unsatisfactory level for crops to grow in an agriculture-based IoT network.

**Bubbles of Trust.** In 2018, Hammi *et al.* \[[46](#9781681088624-C12.html#r46)\] proposed an authentication method where several encapsulated regions containing a number of IoT devices are made. In these regions, the IoT device can trust each other but IoT devices in two different regions cannot access each other. This region is called a bubble in the proposed architecture. All of this is achieved by the use of public blockchain-enabled smart contracts. This bubble comprises two major components, Master and Follower. Master is appointed in the initialization phase (it can be any device) and has a public/private key. Whereas, followers create an elliptic curve private/public key and have a ticket in the form of structure data type detailing the follower’s group ID, Object ID, Public Address, and a Signature created using master’s private key. In summary, in the initialization phase master selects a group ID and provides the object with its signed tickets. Master then relays a transaction, which consists of the master’s identifier (Object ID) and group identifier (Group ID) of the group to be created, and upon the verification of the uniqueness of both the aforementioned identifiers, the bubble is created. After the master creates the bubble, followers relay their transaction to join their desired bubbles. Here, the uniqueness of the follower’s identifier (Object ID) is verified by the smart contract along with the verification of its ticket, which is done by using the master’s public key. Upon successful first transaction of a follower, the authenticity of the follower is established and a ticket is not needed for authentication.

 

#### 3.2.2. Security Solutions for Big Data

**Ownership Management.** There have been many attempts to do ownership management right and there have been some successful cases and unsuccessful ones. The protocol generated by Ascribe called Secure Public Online Ownership Ledger (SPOOL) is listed in \[[7](#9781681088624-C12.html#r7)\] authors. SPOOL is a protocol, which is used to record the rightful ownership of public digital content. They also allow the creator to make certain rules regarding their content like a fixed number of editions. These editions are kept in BigchainDB \[[47](#9781681088624-C12.html#r47)\] as one and hashed for the blockchain. Now, when the creator sells one of their editions a transaction is initiated with the information of hash value, edition, and new ownership. The transaction is then signed by the creator and relayed. In addition, the work can be tracked if the inquirer knows the content’s hash value using a blockchain explorer. However, due to some underlying infrastructure problems, Ascribe had to create a new protocol called as Ocean protocol in 2017. It is a decentralized data exchange protocol, which uses smart contracts, blockchain technology, tokens and unlocks data for AI \[[48](#9781681088624-C12.html#r48)\]. It empowers the creator with more control over their content.

Monegraph is another implementation using blockchain that creates a platform for the artist to trade their work with correct ownership management. It allows the artists to make a customized license certificate, which includes the usage requirements of the art. History of the ownership of the said art is stored in the blockchain and other essential data about the art is stored in document databases.

**Decentralization, Distributed, and Immutable.** The main issues of big data like data growth, security, and validity can be solved by implementing blockchain. Blockchain has key characteristics of data immutability, which helps confirm that data was not modified ensuring data validity \[[49](#9781681088624-C12.html#r49)\]. Data security is provided by the blockchain network as every transaction is recorded and a possible data breach happening is close to null, as there is no single point of failure due to its decentralization. The distributed nature of a blockchain ensures efficient handling of data. One of the solutions offered for proper authentication is decentralized authentication, which uses keys generated by elliptic curve cryptography replacing the traditional method of username and passwords. The only password used by the user is for him/her to access the private key provided that the user could only use that in their own device. The protocol used for identification is based on irrefutable identity verification using public-key-based digital signatures \[[49](#9781681088624-C12.html#r49)\].

 

## 4\. RESULTS

In this section, an evaluation of the veracity of the security threats to IoT and Big Data is done. The two cases that taken here are ransomware and botnets. Ransomware has become more of a threat to enterprises rather than the consumer. As we can see in Fig. ([**2**](#9781681088624-C12.html#f2)), a particular case of ransomware ryuk has run rampant in the year 2019.

![](Images/9781681088624-C12_F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
Ransomware detected for the year 2019. 

Overall, there has been a decrease in the ransomware attacks on consumer networks, but the financial damages incurred are still heavy as there has been a 29% increase from Q2 of 2019 to Q3 of 2019 in ransom paid.

The number of suspected botnets was on the rise too in 2019. As seen in (Fig. [**3**](#9781681088624-C12.html#f3)), for the year 2019 the number of suspected botnet IP’s in the most affected country china increased 3 times from the start of the year to the end.

![](Images/9781681088624-C12_F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Suspected botnet IP’s by countries for the year 2019.  

## CONCLUSION

Blockchain provides us with unique solutions to combat a vast amount of security issues. The technology envisions and revolutionizes data management in such a manner that the user can be more in control of the data with the added benefit of the data being protected. We can conclude from the results that threats like botnets, ransomware, and other security threats are still affecting the current status quo of interconnected devices and networks.

In this paper, some of the major security flaws that exist with the current implementation of IoT and Big data are being discussed. Thereafter, some proposed frameworks and architecture were presented as a solution to the aforementioned security issues. However, there are still some challenges in the implementation of blockchain itself like a proper consensus algorithm that does not use too many computational resources or a proper storage facility for ledgers. Thus, a blockchain should only be integrated with the current technologies if it is economically feasible and provides better security.

 

## FUTURE WORK

In the near future, the majority of the field pertaining to verification and authentication via the internet will look toward blockchain as a solution. It will come with a better implementation of the blockchain to their respective fields. It is worth noting that none of the examples given are based on blockchain proposals for IoT security systems which offers complete defense against all future risks and attacks to health. In fact, the realistic application of such defense technologies is also a matter of the future. Some challenges remain, such as consensus models, mining block computational costs, and transaction validation. Blockchain applications often offer solutions, which require significant modifications or complete replacement of existing systems. Therefore, the transfer is not going to be easy and quick. However, we are still in the early stages of blockchain development, and eventually, these obstacles will be overcome, paving the way for many exciting possibilities.
